<summary>Agent "Trace zwave-js-ui write guarantee" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Short answer:** No. When the zwave-js-ui provider's set or clear returns successfully, the write is not guaranteed to be on the lock. The lock may only have received the command, or it may have explicitly rejected it. So a read right after a successful write that disagrees with it is not necessarily stale.

The reason is that zwave-js-ui reports `success: true` whenever the driver call doesn't throw. The driver returns quietly, without throwing, on several outcomes that are not "applied", and LCM never looks at the result for set or clear.

Versions read: LCM `a2c2157f`; zwave-js-ui `upstream/master@2a3a415d` (package name `zwave-js-ui`, pins `zwave-js` 15.31.1 at package.json:107); node-zwave-js `v15.31.1@48332f7f`. The files I cite are unchanged on `upstream/master@e55b2431`.

**Provider:** zwave_js_ui

**SET path:**
1. `LCM@a2c2157f:providers/zwave_js_ui.py:1276-1306`: `async_set_credential` calls `_async_user_code_command("set", [slot, 1, pin])`, then pushes `known(pin)` and returns `CONFIRMED` without condition.
2. `zwave_js_ui.py:1205-1218`: calls the `sendCommand` api with `{nodeId, commandClass: 99, endpoint: 0}, "set", args`. `response["result"]` is returned, and the set path throws it away.
3. `zwave_js_ui.py:1054-1153`: publishes `&lt;api_base&gt;/api/sendCommand/set` with a nonce and waits up to 60s (`API_CALL_TIMEOUT`, line 60). Only `success` is checked, at line 1148. A timeout raises `LockDisconnected`.
4. `zwave-js-ui@2a3a415d:api/lib/MqttClient.ts:516-551`: strips `/set` and emits `apiCall`. `Gateway.ts:2320-2343` (`_onApiRequest`) awaits `callApi` and only publishes the response once it has finished.
5. `ZwaveClient.ts:6673-6719` (`callApi`): `success:false` only if the call throws. Anything it returns, including a Supervision Fail result, becomes `success:true` plus `result`.
6. `ZwaveClient.ts:6605-6666` (`sendCommand`): calls `endpoint.commandClasses[99].set(...)` directly and returns the raw result. It does not go through `setValue`.
7. `node-zwave-js@48332f7f:packages/cc/src/cc/UserCodeCC.ts:579-614`: `UserCodeCCAPI.set` (or `setMany` at 618-719 for v2+) builds `UserCodeCCSet` / `ExtendedUserCodeSet`, both marked `@useSupervision()` (1428, 2210). It returns `SupervisionResult | undefined`.
8. `packages/zwave-js/src/lib/driver/Driver.ts:8024-8093` (`sendCommand`) and `7984-8013` (`sendSupervisedCommand`): this layer decides what "success" actually means:
   - If Supervision is usable (`SupervisionCC.ts:236-265`), the driver returns whatever status the device reports: Success, Fail or Working (`Supervision.ts:5-10`).
   - If no Supervision Report arrives, `sendCommandInternal` swallows `Controller_NodeTimeout` (Driver.ts:7959-7975). `sendSupervisedCommand` then returns `undefined` (8002), and `sendCommand` passes that through (8052-8054).
   - If the node doesn't support Supervision, or reports NoSupport, the command is sent plain (8057-8068) and the call returns once the node acknowledges the frame (`MessageGenerators.ts:248-265`).
   - If the node never acknowledges the frame, `Controller_CallbackNOK` is thrown (Driver.ts:6967-6973). That becomes `success:false` and `LockOperationFailed`.
9. No driver read-back happens on this path. The verify-and-poll hooks (`UserCodeCC.ts:422-470`) run only from `Node.setValue` (`Node.ts:577`). The Set commands don't update the driver's value cache either: the only cache updates are on Reports (1586, 2346).

**SET guarantee on a successful return:** DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY. It is effectively DEVICE_APPLIED only when Supervision is in use and the device answered Success. LCM can't tell which case happened, because it discards `result`. In detail:
- **Supervision Success:** DEVICE_APPLIED.
- **Supervision Working:** the device has accepted it but hasn't finished.
- **Supervision Fail:** the device rejected it, yet the call still returns success.
- **Supervision Report never arrived** (`undefined`): DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY.
- **No Supervision support:** DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY.
- **ApplicationStatus Rejected** (Driver.ts:8070-8076): turned into a Fail result, so it also returns success.

**CLEAR path + guarantee:** same layers 2-8. The entry point is `zwave_js_ui.py:1308-1319`, which calls `"clear", [slot]`, pushes `empty()` and returns True. In the driver, `UserCodeCCAPI.clear` (`UserCodeCC.ts:726-756`) sends `UserCodeCCSet` with status Available, or `setMany` for v2+. It uses the same supervised send, so the guarantee is the same: DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY, and DEVICE_APPLIED only on a Supervision Success LCM never inspects.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found:**
1. **Supervision Fail.** Driver.ts:8051-8054 returns `{status: 2}`, `ZwaveClient.ts:6708-6712` wraps it as `success:true`, and LCM returns CONFIRMED (`zwave_js_ui.py:1300-1306`).
2. **ApplicationStatus Rejected,** which the driver turns into Fail (Driver.ts:8070-8076). Same outcome as case 1.
3. **Supervision Report timeout.** The node acknowledged the frame but never sent a status. NodeTimeout is swallowed (Driver.ts:7959-7975), `undefined` is returned (8002), and the api reports success.
4. **Supervision Working.** The status is returned as soon as it arrives, because `requestStatusUpdates` is false (Driver.ts:7986-8012). The device hasn't finished applying the write.
5. **No Supervision.** Success means only that the node acknowledged the frame (MessageGenerators.ts:248-265).

Whether real locks produce Fail, Working or a dropped report in practice is UNVERIFIED. I found no device sources.

**Immediate read after a successful write: can it show the pre-write state?** Yes. LCM's poll is a live `UserCodeCCAPI.get` to the device (`UserCodeCC.ts:533-575`, via `zwave_js_ui.py:1254-1274`), not a cached value, so it reflects what the device says. Four ways it can show the old state:
1. **The write never landed** (cases 1-5 above). The read is then the truth, not stale.
2. **Wrong report matched to the read.** `UserCodeCCGet` is declared with `@expectedCCResponse(UserCodeCCReport)` and no matching rule (`UserCodeCC.ts:1636-1637`). `CommandClass.ts:1048-1085` then accepts any `UserCodeCCReport` from that node, whatever slot it is for. A late report from an earlier timed-out Get, or an unsolicited report, can be taken as this read's answer. `get` then drops `userId` (`UserCodeCC.ts:573`), so neither the driver nor LCM can spot the mismatch.
3. **Stale value topic on the push path.** The Set doesn't update the driver's value cache, so the `userCode`/`userIdStatus` value topics keep the pre-write value until a Report comes in. If the user has MQTT retain enabled (`MqttClient.ts:300`, `Gateway.ts:2117-2130`), a resubscribe replays that old value into `_process_user_code_value` → `_confirm_slot` (`zwave_js_ui.py:584-645`). The clear case is partly guarded at 627-641; the set case, where an old code is replayed, is not.
4. **Get timeout.** This comes back as unreadable, not pre-write (`_project_user_code_result(None)`, line 308).

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED classification match what the layers guarantee?** No, it over-claims. The docstring at `zwave_js_ui.py:1289-1291` ("success here means the lock acknowledged the write") holds only when the device returned Supervision Success. CONFIRMED is also returned for Fail, Working, an unanswered supervised set, and an unsupervised set. The `WriteResult` docs (`domain/credentials.py:241-262`) say CONFIRMED on a push provider is "the lock's word", with nothing left pending. For Fail and timeout that is false; for unsupervised it is at most "received".

The information needed to fix this is already in the api response. `response["result"]` holds the `SupervisionResult` (`ZwaveClient.ts:6711`), and LCM drops it at `zwave_js_ui.py:1218` / `1300-1305`:
- `status 255` (Success) → CONFIRMED.
- `status 2` (Fail) → a write failure.
- `status 1` (Working) or a missing result → OPTIMISTIC, left pending.

I found no case where it under-claims.

The direct answer to your design question: a read that disagrees with a just-completed write should not automatically be treated as stale. It is only safely stale when the result was Supervision Success, and even then case 2 (a wrong report matched to the read) makes it ambiguous.

**UNVERIFIED layers:**
- What locks and firmware actually do on receiving Set: whether they support Supervision for User Code, whether they reply Working or Fail, and whether they send unsolicited User Code Reports. No device sources were read.
- HA MQTT and broker delivery between LCM and zwave-js-ui. Not traced beyond LCM's own code.
- I did not trace the driver's retry of a "busy" response to the end. It surfaces as `Controller_NodeTimeout` (MessageGenerators.ts:163-167), which the swallow at Driver.ts:7961-7975 would absorb if it reaches it.</result>

<summary>Agent "Trace Z-Wave JS write guarantee" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>The answer is no. A successful return does not guarantee the lock applied the write, on either set or clear. A read right after one can show the pre-write state, both from a fresh device read and, through LCM's own reconcile read, from the cache. So a disagreeing read right after a write cannot automatically be treated as stale. Everything below is read-only; nothing was edited, pulled or checked out.

**SHAs read.** The library checkouts' `origin` remotes are mostly your forks, so I read `upstream` where one exists.
- LCM `main` at a2c2157f.
- HA core `upstream/dev` at 970de640. Its `lock_helpers.py` is byte-identical to the installed copy in the venv (2026.9.0b4). Note `requirements_dev.txt` asks for &gt;=2026.9.4, so the venv is behind.
- zwave-js-server-python `upstream/main` at 7b983a70 (0.74.0+7). Its `access_control.py` is identical to the installed 0.73.1.
- zwave-js-server `origin/master` at 400fd940 (this is the real upstream). It depends on zwave-js ^15.31.0.
- node-zwave-js `upstream/master` at e55b2431 (v15.31.1+5). Your fork's `origin/master` is stale (v15.20.1), so I did not use it.

---

**Provider:** zwave_js

**SET path**
1. LCM@a2c2157f `providers/zwave_js.py:646` calls `lock_helpers.async_set_credential`. It returns OPTIMISTIC at `:676` when HA raises translation key `credential_rejected_unknown`. Any other HA error is a rejection (`:681-694`). On success it does a reconcile read at `:698` (fresh single-slot get, User Code CC locks only, best-effort, `:951-990`), pushes `SlotCredential.known(pin)` at `:700`, and returns CONFIRMED at `:701`.
2. HA@970de640 `components/zwave_js/lock_helpers.py:577-580` calls `node.access_control.set_credential`. Anything other than `OK` raises; `ERROR_UNKNOWN` maps to `credential_rejected_unknown` (`:121`, `:135-144`).
3. zwave-js-server-python@7b983a70 `model/access_control.py:893-911` sends `access_control.set_credential` with `wait_for_result=True`. `model/endpoint.py:189` awaits the result and `client.py:113-133` awaits the future with no timeout. It returns the driver's `SetCredentialResult` as-is.
4. zwave-js-server@400fd940 `src/lib/endpoint/access_control/message_handler.ts:153-160` awaits `endpoint.accessControl.setCredential(...)` and passes the result through.
5. Driver@e55b2431 `feature-apis/AccessControl.ts:1126-1238`. It uses User Credential CC if the node supports it and doesn't report 0 users, otherwise User Code CC (`:269-288`).
   - **User Credential CC:** `api.setCredential` (`cc/UserCredentialCC.ts:501-518`) sends Credential Set, which expects a Credential Report (`:2617-2622`). Commands that expect a response are never supervised (`SupervisionCC.ts:244`). The report type maps to OK for Added, Modified, Deleted or Unchanged (`AccessControl.ts:158-167`). No report (timeout) gives `Error_Unknown` (`:161`).
   - **User Code CC:** `api.set` (`UserCodeCC.ts:579-614`) is marked `@useSupervision()` (`:1428`, `:2210`). The driver supervises it if the node supports Supervision CC (`Driver.ts:8044-8055`, `SupervisionCC.ts:253-263`). Then:
     - No supervision result (unsupervised, or the Supervision Report timed out; `Driver.ts:7959-7964`, `:8002`): the driver does a verification GET and succeeds if status and code match (`AccessControl.ts:1186-1194`).
     - Supervision SUCCESS or WORKING: success with no GET; the driver writes the new value into its value DB itself (`:1195-1199`, persistence added in v15.25.2, commit 5239ec3c6).
     - Supervision FAIL, or Application Status Rejected (mapped to FAIL at `Driver.ts:8070-8077`): a GET, then success only on an exact match. Otherwise `Error_Unknown`, except `ModifyRejectedLocationEmpty` (`AccessControl.ts:1200-1218`).
   - The `setValue` path with `verifyChanges`/`schedulePoll` (`UserCodeCC.ts:454-468`) is **not** on this path; the access-control API calls CC API methods directly.

**SET guarantee on successful return:** mixed, depending on how the lock is controlled.
- User Credential CC: **DEVICE_APPLIED**. The lock's own Credential Report states the outcome.
- User Code CC with Supervision SUCCESS: **DEVICE_APPLIED**. The driver docs define it as "The device has executed the command successfully" (`docs/api/node.md:187`).
- User Code CC with Supervision WORKING: **DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY**. Docs: "The device has accepted the command and is working on it" (`node.md:175`). The driver counts WORKING as success (`core/src/definitions/Supervision.ts:35-45`). It doesn't request status updates, so it never hears the final outcome (`Driver.ts:8004-8012`).
- User Code CC without Supervision, or with a timed-out Supervision Report: **VERIFIED_BY_READBACK**. One weakness: on a version 1 lock, an all-`*` read-back plus matching status counts as confirmed (`AccessControl.ts:245-265`), so the code itself is not verified.
- Separately, if the slot was Disabled, the code is written but stays Disabled (`AccessControl.ts:1168-1178`).

**CLEAR path + guarantee**
1. LCM `zwave_js.py:710` calls `lock_helpers.async_delete_credential`. Success returns True with no reconcile read; LCM relies on the driver clearing its cache (`:719-722`). Any HA error, including `ERROR_UNKNOWN`, becomes `LockOperationFailed`; it is never optimistic (`:718-725`).
2. HA `lock_helpers.py:606-609`: anything other than `OK` raises.
3. Python layer `access_control.py:913-929`; server layer `message_handler.ts:162-169`; both pass through.
4. Driver `AccessControl.ts:1254-1336`:
   - User Credential CC: a Delete-operation Credential Set, mapped from the report as above (`:1270-1285`).
   - User Code CC: `api.clear` (`UserCodeCC.ts:726-756`), supervised. Supervision SUCCESS or WORKING is success with no GET (`AccessControl.ts:1307-1308`). Otherwise a GET, success if the slot reads Available (`:1309-1315`). On success the driver resets its value DB to Available/"" (`:1316-1317`, `:1706-1727`). If the GET times out, the result is `Error_Unknown`.

Guarantee: User Credential CC is **DEVICE_APPLIED**. User Code CC is **DEVICE_APPLIED** with SUCCESS, **DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY** with WORKING, and **VERIFIED_BY_READBACK** unsupervised.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
1. Supervision WORKING on User Code CC set or clear returns OK before the lock has finished (`Supervision.ts:42-43`; `AccessControl.ts:1195`, `:1307`; `Driver.ts:8005`). For a clear, the driver also marks the slot Available in its value DB.
2. Version 1 obfuscation leniency: if the slot already held an Enabled code, a lock that ignored the write still reads back `******` with status Enabled. That passes `userCodeReadBackConfirmsSet` (`AccessControl.ts:258-262`), and LCM returns CONFIRMED.
3. A slot that was Disabled gets the new code written but stays Disabled, and the call returns OK (`AccessControl.ts:1174-1178`). Applied, but not usable.
4. Theoretical, User Credential CC only: the response matcher correlates on credential type and slot only, and accepts any Duplicate report (`UserCredentialCC.ts:2598-2614`). An unsolicited report for the same slot arriving during the wait could be taken as the answer. I found no observed instance.

**Immediate read after a successful write: can it show pre-write state?** Yes.
- **Fresh device read (hard refresh, LCM's reconcile GET):** after a WORKING, the lock may not have applied the write yet. Whether real locks send WORKING for user-code commands is UNVERIFIED.
- **LCM's own reconcile read can push the cache back:** after a successful User Code CC set, `_async_uc_reconcile_value_db` (`zwave_js.py:698`, `:951-976`) does a fresh GET right away. The driver stores every received report in its value DB (`Driver.ts:4279-4281`, `:6636-6645`). So if the lock answers with pre-write state (the WORKING case), that overwrites what the driver had just written (`AccessControl.ts:1199`). LCM then pushes `known(pin)` to its coordinator anyway (`:700`), and the next `async_get_users`, which reads cached data (`zwave_js.py:287-288`), returns the pre-write slot.
- **Older drivers:** before v15.25.2, a supervised SUCCESS was never written to the value DB (v15.25.1 `AccessControl.ts` setCredential path). Cached reads stay pre-write unless LCM's reconcile read lands.
- **Version 1 masking locks:** the read-back stores `******` in the cache, which LCM reads as unreadable, not as the PIN (`zwave_js.py:251-259`).
- **Clear:** a cached read shows cleared (driver reset), but a fresh read after a WORKING could still show the old code.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED/OPTIMISTIC classification match what the layers guarantee?**
- **Over-claims CONFIRMED:** the WORKING case, the version 1 masked-modify case and the Disabled-slot case all return CONFIRMED. Treating a contradicting read as stale would be wrong in exactly these cases.
- **Under-claims (OPTIMISTIC is too generous):** `ERROR_UNKNOWN` covers more than "write-only or masked lock, the write actually succeeded" (the docstring's claim at `zwave_js.py:631-642`). It also covers:
  - the lock explicitly rejecting the write (Supervision FAIL or Application Status Rejected, read-back mismatch; `AccessControl.ts:1185`, `:1200-1218`);
  - a verification GET that timed out (`:1187` returns undefined);
  - a slow lock still showing the old value at verification time;
  - a missing Credential Report on User Credential CC (`:161`).

  OPTIMISTIC can mean the lock said no.
- **Asymmetric:** the same `ERROR_UNKNOWN` on clear raises `LockOperationFailed` (`zwave_js.py:718-725`), while on set it is optimistic.
- **Matches:** User Credential CC reports, Supervision SUCCESS, and unsupervised exact-match read-back are correctly CONFIRMED / True.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- Whether any real lock firmware sends Supervision WORKING for User Code Set or Extended User Code Set.
- Whether a lock may send WORKING when status updates weren't requested (Supervision spec text isn't in the repo).
- Whether a lock's SUCCESS or User Credential CC report always means the change is actually in effect (firmware honesty).
- Which driver version runs in your production deployment, and so whether the v15.25.2 cache-persist fix applies there.</result>

<summary>Agent "Trace Zigbee2MQTT write guarantee" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I traced every layer. The short answer is no: a successful return from LCM's Zigbee2MQTT write tells you nothing about the lock, so a read that disagrees right after it can be true and must not be treated as stale.

**Provider: zigbee2mqtt**

Versions read: LCM main@a2c2157f, zigbee2mqtt origin/dev@3ee6b54a, zigbee-herdsman-converters master@d436575f (zhc), zigbee-herdsman master@e89385f2 (zh), Home Assistant core 2026.9.0b4 as installed in `.venv`. Z2M dev pins zhc 26.118.0 and zh 11.0.2 (`package.json:60-61`). I read upstream master, not those exact tags.

**SET path**
1. LCM, `zigbee2mqtt.py:474-491` (a2c2157f): publishes `{"pin_code":{"user":N,"user_type":"unrestricted","pin_code":pin,"user_enabled":true}}` to `&lt;device&gt;/set`. It subscribes to nothing that would carry a reply. Lines 516-519 then push `SlotCredential.known(pin)` into the coordinator itself and return `CONFIRMED`.
2. HA MQTT, `.venv/.../mqtt/client.py:153` uses QoS 0 by default. Line 771 hands the message to paho, and line 781 waits for the "mid" (message ID) callback. At 1506-1509 an ACK timeout only logs a warning and still returns normally. Only a nonzero paho result code (1488-1495) or MQTT being disabled (160-165) raises.
3. Z2M, `lib/mqtt.ts:194-198` + `lib/eventBus.ts:251-263` (3ee6b54a): the message is emitted synchronously to async listeners that nobody awaits. That means /set and /get messages are handled concurrently, with no reply to the publisher.
4. Z2M, `lib/extension/publish.ts:250` awaits `convertSet`. Line 253 publishes optimistic state only if the converter returns `result.state`, and `pincode_lock` returns none. Any thrown error is caught at 282-286 and goes only to `logger.error`.
5. Z2M, `lib/extension/bridge.ts:62-69,82-91`: errors reach MQTT only as uncorrelated text on `&lt;base&gt;/bridge/logging` (`"Publish 'set' 'pin_code' to '&lt;name&gt;' failed: ..."`). LCM does not subscribe to that topic; `zigbee2mqtt.py:413` subscribes to the device topic only.
6. zhc, [toZigbee.ts:430-455@d436575f](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman-converters/blob/d436575ffdcf9e7d35a9bfd0a6d45b47fc0e6458/src/converters/toZigbee.ts#L430-L455): `pincode_lock` range-checks the user against `0..pinCodeCount-1` and throws if out of range (440). At 453 it calls `await entity.command("closuresDoorLock","setPinCode",…)` and throws the returned payload away. The generic `m.lock()` uses this converter ([modernExtend.ts:1632](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman-converters/blob/d436575ffdcf9e7d35a9bfd0a6d45b47fc0e6458/src/lib/modernExtend.ts#L1632)), and so does Yale's `lockExtend` ([yale.ts:168-169](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman-converters/blob/d436575ffdcf9e7d35a9bfd0a6d45b47fc0e6458/src/devices/yale.ts#L168-L169)), which sets no response-related options.
7. zh, [cluster.ts:3143-3146](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman/blob/e89385f2233c29c45d46f37f02a25b527d9b01d4/src/zspec/zcl/definition/cluster.ts#L3143-L3146): setPinCode expects response 0x05, `setPinCodeRsp{status}` (3294).
8. zh, [endpoint.ts:956](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman/blob/e89385f2233c29c45d46f37f02a25b527d9b01d4/src/controller/model/endpoint.ts#L956): `command()` calls `zclCommand(..., checkStatus=false, ...)`. Because of that, `checkStatus` (1197-1198, 455-458) never runs, and a response with a non-SUCCESS status resolves normally. Lines 441-449: on a sleepy device whose send fails, the request is queued for later instead of failing.
9. zh ember adapter, [emberAdapter.ts:1961-1965,2052-2063](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman/blob/e89385f2233c29c45d46f37f02a25b527d9b01d4/src/adapter/ember/adapter/emberAdapter.ts#L1961): waits up to 10s for the specific response by sequence number. [oneWaitress.ts:166-182](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman/blob/e89385f2233c29c45d46f37f02a25b527d9b01d4/src/adapter/ember/adapter/oneWaitress.ts#L166) also accepts a Default Response with any status. It rejects on timeout or on delivery failure (82-102).

**SET guarantee on successful return: SENT_ONLY**
- What LCM's return actually means: the HA MQTT client handed the message to paho. Even a missing broker ACK still returns success.
- Nothing changes this. Every lower-layer failure is invisible to LCM: Z2M down, out-of-range slot, Zigbee timeout or delivery failure, and a device response with failure status.
- Inside Z2M (which LCM never sees), a non-throwing `convertSet` means the device sent a response with an unchecked status. That is DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY, not DEVICE_APPLIED.

**CLEAR path + guarantee: SENT_ONLY**
- LCM, `zigbee2mqtt.py:539-575`: publishes `{"pin_code":{"user":N,"user_type":"unrestricted","user_enabled":false,"pin_code":null}}`, pushes `SlotCredential.empty()`, and returns True.
- zhc, toZigbee.ts:442-443: `pin_code == null` sends `clearPinCode{userid}` and ignores `user_enabled`. The response is 0x07 `clearPinCodeRsp{status}` (cluster.ts:3155, 3305).
- Layers 2-5, 8 and 9 are identical to SET, so the status is unchecked here too.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
1. The device answers setPinCode/clearPinCode with a non-zero status. The status is never checked (endpoint.ts:956), Z2M logs nothing, and LCM reports CONFIRMED. I did not check what each status code means in the ZCL spec.
2. A Default Response with an error status satisfies the waiter (oneWaitress.ts:170-173) and is not checked either.
3. Any converter or Zigbee error (out-of-range user at toZigbee.ts:440, 10s timeout, "Delivery failed") goes only to `bridge/logging`, which LCM does not follow (publish.ts:282-286).
4. The broker is up but Z2M is not consuming. A QoS 0, non-retained publish is dropped and LCM still returns CONFIRMED (client.py:1506-1509).
5. `lock_set_pin_code_response`, the only converter that turns a rsp status into state (`last_successful_pincode_save`, [fromZigbee.ts:362-378](https://github.com/Koenkk/zigbee-herdsman-converters/blob/d436575ffdcf9e7d35a9bfd0a6d45b47fc0e6458/src/converters/fromZigbee.ts#L362-L378)), is used only by `src/devices/onesti.ts` (gh code search). It is not used by `m.lock()` or Yale, and LCM ignores it anyway.

**Immediate read after a successful write: yes, it can show the pre-write state, for two separate reasons**
- **The read may be true.** The write may simply have failed (all the cases above). So a disagreeing read is not proof of staleness.
- **The read may be served from Z2M's cache.**
  - Z2M never updates its cached `users` entry on a set, because `pincode_lock` returns no state.
  - With `cache_state` (default true, `settings.ts:108`), every state publish republishes the full deep-merged cache (`controller.ts:385-391`, `state.ts:115-123`). That includes the pre-write `users[N]`.
  - LCM's pending read for slot N is resolved by any `users` payload that contains N (`zigbee2mqtt.py:284-293`). A battery, lock-state or other republish arriving between LCM's /get and the real GetPinCodeRsp therefore answers the read with the pre-write value.
  - The LCM comment there only rules out other slots' entries, not a stale entry for the same slot.
- **Ordering is a partial mitigation, not a guarantee.** The ember adapter runs one job at a time per network address, in FIFO order, and each job holds the slot until its response arrives (queue.ts `#getNext`; emberAdapter.ts:279,1990). A Zigbee GetPinCode issued after the SetPinCode would see its result. But Z2M handles /set and /get concurrently (eventBus.ts:251), so the enqueue order is not guaranteed.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED classification match what the layers guarantee? No, it over-claims.**
- `domain/credentials.py:248-250` says CONFIRMED on a push provider "is the lock's word". `_base.py:2031` says "CONFIRMED when the lock acknowledged the write". `_base.py:1439-1443` treats "the provider's own push" as the lock's word and calls `drop_pending`, so the write is never tracked as unverified.
- For Z2M, that "push" is LCM's own optimistic `_push_credential_update` (`zigbee2mqtt.py:517-519`), made after a fire-and-forget QoS 0 publish. The code comments there admit "no delivery guarantee; hard_refresh mitigates drift".
- The honest classification is OPTIMISTIC: record the write as pending with the value believed, then settle it by a readback.
- That readback is itself contaminated by the cache path above. So the claim at `_mqtt.py:105-107` that "an ordinary read … waits for the lock's own answer" also over-claims.
- **The rule being considered is unsound for this provider:** a read that disagrees with a just-completed write cannot be assumed stale.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- zstack, deCONZ, ZBOSS and ZiGate adapters: I verified only ember's wait and match behaviour.
- The exact pinned dependency tags (zhc 26.118.0, zh 11.0.2): I read master at the SHAs above.
- Whether particular locks (Yale/Assa Abloy or others) send `ProgrammingEventNotification` after a remote set or clear. That event is what drives LCM's refresh at `zigbee2mqtt.py:246-259`, so its timing is unknown.
- What status codes and Default Responses specific firmware sends on rejection, and what the ZCL status values mean.
- MQTT ordering between LCM's /set and /get across different topics at QoS 0.

The upstream sources I downloaded for reading are in `/tmp/lcm_research/`. I made no edits anywhere.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Provider: zha

The short answer: a successful return means the lock sent back a SUCCESS status, as long as the reply actually has a status field. LCM does check that status, but it returns CONFIRMED without one when the reply has no status field, and zigpy can match the wrong frame to a request. That over-claim affects both writes and reads.

Versions read: LCM main a2c2157f. Installed in the venv: zigpy 2.1.0, zha 2.2.0, zha_quirks 2.2.1, homeassistant 2026.9.0b4. I diffed zigpy's `Device.request` and `_maybe_match_response` and the DoorLock response schemas against zigpy dev b4543ff5 (newest release is 2.3.0) and found them unchanged.

**SET path**
1. `providers/zha.py:304-309`@a2c2157f — calls `cluster.set_pin_code(slot, UserStatus.Enabled, UserType.Unrestricted, pin)` directly on the zigpy DoorLock cluster. The cluster is found at `zha.py:172-189` through `device_proxy.device.device`. It bypasses Home Assistant services and zha's lock platform; Home Assistant core only locates the gateway (`homeassistant/components/zha/helpers.py:1165`@2026.9.0b4, `zha/zigbee/device.py:465-467`@2.2.0).
2. `zha.py:310-311` — any exception becomes `LockDisconnected`.
3. `zha.py:318-325` — `if hasattr(result, "status") and result.status != 0` raises `CodeRejectedError`. Otherwise it pushes `SlotCredential.known(pin)` and returns `CONFIRMED`.
4. `zigpy/zcl/__init__.py:1816-1833`@2.1.0 — `__getattr__` turns `set_pin_code` into `command(0x05)`, which calls `request(expect_reply=True)` (`:1735-1758`, `:803-856`).
5. `zigpy/zcl/clusters/closures.py:429-437` — command schema. The reply is `set_pin_code_response` (0x05) with `{"status": foundation.Status}` (`:565-568`).
6. `zigpy/endpoint.py:209-245` hands off to `zigpy/device.py:609-725`. It builds the response key from (endpoint, cluster, flipped direction, TSN), with no command id (`:650-655`). It awaits a future for 5 s, or 28 s for end devices (`:633-636`, `const.py:23-24`), with 2 retries that resend the same TSN (`device.py:56`, `:689-725`). `ParsingError` is raised without a retry (`:711`).
7. `zigpy/application.py:1125-1164` — when `expect_reply=True`, no APS ACK is requested (`tx_options` stays NONE). The only signal that the frame arrived is the ZCL reply.
8. `device.py:887-914`, `:956` — any server-to-client frame on that endpoint and cluster with the same TSN resolves the future. Only `Report_Attributes` is excluded (`:897-903`). A matched frame is not passed on to cluster listeners (`:956-957`).
9. `zcl/__init__.py:720-750` — an unknown cluster command id comes back as raw `bytes` (`:735-739`). A non-zero status never raises in zigpy; it is just returned. Unknown codes deserialize instead of failing: status 2 shows up as `Status.MAC_PAN_ACCESS_DENIED` (through `_missing_`, `foundation.py:64-72`) and status 3 as `undefined_0x03` (checked by running it). Both are non-zero, so LCM rejects them.

**SET guarantee on successful return:** DEVICE_APPLIED, in the sense that the lock replied with ZCL status 0, either in `set_pin_code_response` or in a Default Response (`foundation.py:1288-1290`, which has a `.status` field). It drops to UNVERIFIED when the matched reply has no `status` attribute (see the cases below). Whether status 0 means the PIN is committed and visible to the next read depends on firmware: UNVERIFIED.

**CLEAR path + guarantee**
1. `zha.py:337` calls `cluster.clear_pin_code(slot)`. `:338-339` maps exceptions to `LockDisconnected`. `:346-353` rejects a non-zero status, otherwise pushes `SlotCredential.empty()` and returns True.
2. `closures.py:442-445` is the command (0x07). The reply is `clear_pin_code_response {"status": foundation.Status}` (`:578-581`).
3. Layers 4 and 6–9 are the same as for SET.

Guarantee: DEVICE_APPLIED (status 0), with the same UNVERIFIED drop when the reply has no status.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
- **Reply without a status field returns CONFIRMED.** Matching uses only the TSN (`device.py:650-655`, `:895-910`). Any frame with a colliding TSN therefore resolves the request, and LCM's `hasattr` check (`zha.py:318`, `:346`) lets it through as success. Examples:
  - an unsolicited `operation_event_notification` or `programming_event_notification` from the lock (`closures.py:680-703`), whose own TSN counter happens to equal the pending request's TSN;
  - an unknown or manufacturer cluster command, which arrives as raw bytes (`zcl/__init__.py:735-739`).
  
  In that situation nothing tells us whether the lock even received the command. A side effect: the matched notification never reaches LCM's push listener (`device.py:956-957`).
- **Applied but reported as failed (under-claim):**
  - zigpy resends with the same TSN after a timeout (`device.py:689-725`). If the first attempt was applied and only its reply was lost, a non-zero reply to the resend makes LCM raise `CodeRejectedError` even though the lock holds the PIN. What the device answers to a duplicate set or clear: UNVERIFIED.
  - Any exception raised after the frame went out becomes `LockDisconnected`, though the write may have landed. LCM's 720 s deadline (`zha.py:59-72`, `_base.py:323-331`) sits above zigpy's worst case, so this path is rare.
- I found no case where the lock returns a FAILURE status, nothing raises, and LCM misses it: every non-zero status raises `CodeRejectedError`.

**Immediate read after a successful write: can it show pre-write state?**
Not through any software cache. The read at `zha.py:388` is a live `get_pin_code` ZCL command with a fresh TSN (`device.py:204`), and zigpy caches attributes only, not command replies. LCM also runs reads and writes on a lock one at a time behind `_aio_lock` (`_base.py:313-334`), so LCM's own read cannot be in flight during its write.

It can still show pre-write or wrong state in three ways:
1. **Wrong match on the read.** The same TSN-only matching can resolve a `get_pin_code` with a `programming_event_notification`. That schema also has `user_status` (`closures.py:691-703`), so `_parse_pin_response` (`zha.py:520-524`) accepts it. It does not check `user_id` against the requested slot. An event whose status is Available would mark the slot EMPTY, and an Enabled one with an empty `code` reads as unreadable (`zha.py:446-455`). A clear does the same with an Enabled event: the slot reads as unreadable rather than empty.
2. **The write's success was itself a false match** (the first case in the list above).
3. **The lock answered status 0 before committing the change.** Firmware behavior: UNVERIFIED.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED match what the layers guarantee?**
Mostly yes. Because `supports_push` is True (`zha.py:102-111`), CONFIRMED means "the lock's word, nothing pending" (`credentials.py:241-259`). That holds when the lock replied with status 0.
- **Over-claim:** CONFIRMED with no status present (`zha.py:318`, `:346`). The fix is to require a status: treat a missing status as OPTIMISTIC, or raise.
- **Under-claim:** a non-zero status after a zigpy retry, and timeouts that land after the frame was sent.
- **The read side matters for your decision:** a disagreeing read right after a write is not automatically stale. It can be a mis-matched frame, so a read that disagrees with a just-confirmed write should not be counted as a definite contradiction. Also, the read never checks `user_id`.
- **Aside:** zha's own lock platform writes to slot−1 (`zha/application/platforms/lock/__init__.py:156-183`@2.2.0) and ignores the result, while LCM writes to `slot` itself. A code set through zha's own lock action therefore lands one slot below where LCM looks for it.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- Radio libraries (bellows, znp, deconz, xbee) `send_packet` — not read. It doesn't change the guarantee, since success depends on the ZCL reply.
- Lock firmware: whether status 0 means committed and readable, how it answers a resent set or clear, and whether it sends a programming event before or after its reply.
- ZCL specification meaning of the DoorLock status codes 2 and 3 — not read. zigpy's labels for them are generic `foundation.Status` names.
- The Home Assistant core checkout's origin/dev — not compared. I used the venv's 2026.9.0b4, which is only used to look up the device and is not on the write path.</result>

<summary>Agent "Trace Matter write guarantee" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>Short answer: when a set or clear returns successfully, the lock itself has said the command worked, so it was not merely sent. That is still not proof that it took effect, though. The one readback LCM does after a set only checks which user owns the credential, and LCM ignores that readback's "does not exist" answer. Persistence on the device is unverified. And a read made right after a successful write can show the pre-write state, through two concrete paths in the server and in LCM.

Provider: matter

**Versions read:** LCM a2c2157f; HA 2026.9.0b4 in the venv (its `lock_helpers.py` is identical to the local `origin/dev` 69795208, which I did not fetch); `matter-python-client` 1.4.0, the client for the matter.js-based OHF server (`METADATA` names matter-js/matterjs-server, not python-matter-server); matterjs-server main dea00a85 (`handleInvoke`/`#invokeCommand` behaviour also confirmed at tag v1.4.0, 83b79084); connectedhomeip (Matter reference SDK) master e36de71f.

**SET path**
1. `providers/matter.py:972-1095`@a2c2157f: `async_set_credential` reads the user's PIN index live (`_find_pin_credential_index_for_user` → GetUser walk), then calls `_send_set_credential` (`:895-948`), which wraps HA's `set_lock_credential`.
2. `homeassistant/components/matter/lock_helpers.py:674-783`@2026.9.0b4: sends GetCredentialStatus to choose Add or Modify (`:709-743`). Then it sends DoorLock **SetCredential** as a timed invoke, 10 s (`:753-768`). It **does check `SetCredentialResponse.status`**: anything other than 0–3 → `SetCredentialFailedError` with `unknown(&lt;code&gt;)` (`:770-777`). The returned `credential_index` is the index HA asked for, not one the device reported (`:779-783`). `userIndex` and `nextCredentialIndex` are passed up; LCM never uses them.
3. `matter_server/client/client.py:576-601`, `:764-781`, `:857-863`@1.4.0: `send_device_command` → `send_command` waits on a future with no client-side timeout. A success message resolves to `msg.result`; an error message raises `exception_from_error_code(code)(details)`. On disconnect, pending futures are cancelled (`:835-841`).
4. matterjs-server `packages/ws-controller/src/server/WebSocketControllerHandler.ts:1240-1283`@dea00a85: `device_command` → `handleInvoke`. Any thrown error becomes an error reply whose code is `UnknownError` unless it is a `ServerError`; the message text goes in `details` (`:862-870`).
5. matterjs-server `packages/ws-controller/src/controller/ControllerCommandHandler.ts:1071-1094`@dea00a85: a non-success IM status throws `StatusResponseError` (`:1085`); IM success with no payload returns `undefined`/null; a command response returns the payload unchanged (`:1089-1090`). The server never looks at the payload's status. It also merges identical in-flight invokes into one (`:1130-1166`).
6. Matter semantics, from the reference SDK `src/app/clusters/door-lock-server/CodegenIntegration.cpp`@e36de71f: `setCredentialCommandHandler` (`:660`) sends SetCredentialResponse with a door-lock status. Duplicate PIN → kDuplicate (`:731-765`); Add → `createCredential` result (`:797-807`); Modify → `modifyCredential` result (`:809-853`). Bad index, wrong owner and wrong fabric all return **kInvalidField 0x85** (`:710,784,794,818,831`).
7. After the write, LCM sends GetCredentialStatus for the written index (`matter.py:1097-1194`). If another user owns it, LCM clears it and raises; otherwise it pushes `unreadable` and returns CONFIRMED (`:1093-1095`).

**SET guarantee on successful return:** DEVICE_APPLIED, meaning the device's own word: IM success plus `SetCredentialResponse.status == kSuccess`. It is **not** VERIFIED_BY_READBACK:
- If the GetCredentialStatus read fails, the error is logged at debug level and the write stands (`matter.py:1142-1151`).
- If the read answers `credentialExists=false`, the owner is None and LCM returns success anyway (`:1162`).
- Whether the device has actually stored the PIN when it answers success is UNVERIFIED. In the reference SDK the response is sent after the vendor storage call `emberAfPluginDoorLockSetCredential` returns a bool; what vendor firmware does inside that call is unknown.

**CLEAR path + guarantee**
1. `matter.py:1196-1227`: reads the PIN index live; if there is none, returns False with no write. Otherwise it calls `clear_lock_credential` through `_invoke_sdk` (`:420-449`), pushes `empty`, and returns True.
2. `lock_helpers.py:786-812`: DoorLock **ClearCredential**, timed invoke. It has no response payload, so only the IM status counts.
3. Same client and server layers as SET (steps 3–5). A non-success IM status becomes `UnknownError` with the status only in the message text, and `_invoke_sdk` turns it into `LockDisconnected`.
4. Reference SDK `clearCredential` (`CodegenIntegration.cpp:3013-3166`): returns IM Success after storage is updated. If it was the user's only credential, the **user is deleted too** (`:3080-3101`).

Guarantee: DEVICE_APPLIED, but only in the sense "the device says that index no longer holds that credential type". The reference SDK also returns **Success when nothing was cleared**: an empty slot (`:3045-3052`) or a different credential type at that index (`:3054-3062`).

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
- ClearCredential on an index that is already empty, or holds a different type, returns IM Success (`CodegenIntegration.cpp:3045-3062`). LCM then reports `changed=True` and adds the slot to `_cleared_slots` (`_base.py:1556-1585`), even though nothing was removed. If the index came from a stale read and LCM's PIN actually sits elsewhere, the clear "succeeds" and the PIN stays.
- SET with IM and response success, but GetCredentialStatus then reports the credential missing or the read fails: still CONFIRMED (`matter.py:1142-1151`, `:1162`).
- An IM success with no response entries would become null; for ClearCredential that is indistinguishable from success (`ControllerCommandHandler.ts:1079-1093`, loop exits with `undefined`). Whether matter.js can actually produce this is UNVERIFIED. For SET, a null response becomes `unknown(None)`, which is not a success.
- Not a success case, but related, in the other direction: a LCM budget timeout (`_base.py:322-329`) or a client disconnect (`client.py:839-840`, CancelledError) fires after the command was sent. LCM reports failure, but the write may have landed.

**Immediate read after a successful write**
Yes, it can show the pre-write state. LCM's reads are live device commands (GetUser at `lock_helpers.py:498`, GetCredentialStatus at `:833`), not the server's attribute cache, so cache or subscription lag is not the mechanism. The real paths are:
- **Server-side merge:** matterjs-server returns the *in-flight* result for an identical command (`ControllerCommandHandler.ts:1130-1140`). A GetUser(n) issued after the write can get the answer of a GetUser(n) started before it. This can happen because LCM's event-dispatch tasks read the lock outside the per-lock turn lock: `matter.py:1562-1563` → `_raw_lock_users` at `:1586`, not via `_execute_rate_limited`.
- **Concurrent walk:** `get_lock_users` is a multi-command GetUser walk, not an atomic snapshot. A walk running alongside the write (the same event tasks, or HA core's own lock management) can straddle it.
- **Device-side delay:** whether a device can answer success before its storage reflects the write is UNVERIFIED.

So a disagreeing read is not provably stale, but it is not proof of a contradiction either unless the read was issued after the write returned and could not have been merged.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED classification match what the layers guarantee?**
- **SET:** mostly matches. LCM requires IM success and a success response status, which is the device's statement that it applied the write. It over-claims only if CONFIRMED is read as "verified": the owner check is best-effort and ignores a "not found" answer.
- **SET retries vs rejections:** status 133 (0x85) is treated as transient (`matter.py:93-114`). In the reference SDK, 0x85 is kInvalidField, a definitive rejection (wrong owner or fabric, bad index). The installed definitions also type the response status as the global status enum (`chip/clusters/cluster_defs/DoorLock.py:1025`, `Globals.py:187-224`), which includes AlreadyExists 0xD0 and ResourceExhausted 0x89. HA only names 0–3, so those also come through as `unknown(...)`. These are wrongly sent to retry, not false successes.
- **CLEAR:** `changed=True` over-claims "something was removed", because Success also covers no-op clears. The end state (index empty of PIN) does match.
- **Docstrings:** the comments say Matter emits no LockUserChange for LCM-initiated writes (`matter.py:989-991`, `:1206-1208`). The reference SDK does emit it for remote add, modify and clear (`CodegenIntegration.cpp:2600,2686,3160-3164`). Behaviour may vary by device (UNVERIFIED), so an event can arrive after the write returns.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- Vendor lock firmware: whether storage is done when success is returned, and whether it follows the reference SDK's no-op-Success and status choices.
- matter.js `node.interaction.invoke` internals: whether an empty response is possible, and its timeout and retransmit behaviour. I did not read the `@matter/*` packages.
- The legacy python-matter-server path: I read only `server/device_controller.py:688-724`@4c820ed1, which forwards to the CHIP SDK `send_command`. I did not read how the SDK wrapper maps IM errors.
- The Matter spec prose itself (CSA-gated, not read). I used the reference SDK and the installed cluster definitions as stand-ins.
- Which matterjs-server version the user actually runs. I read main and v1.4.0 only.</result>

<result>## Schlage

**Provider:** schlage. LCM is at a2c2157f. HA core is at `upstream/dev` 970de640; `homeassistant/components/schlage/lock.py` is the same on origin/dev 69795208. pyschlage is 2026.9.0 @ 7520451b, the version upstream/dev pins; origin/dev still pins 2026.7.0. pyschlage is not installed in `.venv`.

**SET path**
1. `providers/schlage.py:382-431`: under the sequence lock it calls `get_codes`, then `delete_code` for the existing name and the tagged name (failures suppressed, :403-406), then `add_code`. Any error containing "already exists" is swallowed (:408-417). It always returns `CONFIRMED`.
2. `providers/_base.py:2238-2266` calls `hass.services.async_call(blocking=True)`. An OSError becomes `LockDisconnected`; a `HomeAssistantError` becomes `LockOperationFailed`.
3. HA `helpers/service.py:726` drops unavailable entities. If none are left and no response was requested, it returns `None` with no error (:765-770). The Schlage entity is available only while `self._lock.connected` (`schlage/entity.py:44-52`).
4. HA `schlage/lock.py:117-134` (`add_code`):
   - re-fetches codes from the cloud (:106-115);
   - raises `ServiceValidationError` on a duplicate name (:79-92) or a duplicate PIN value (:94-104);
   - runs `add_access_code` in an executor, then `coordinator.async_request_refresh()`.
5. pyschlage `lock.py:388-398` → `code.py:275-308` `save()`:
   - `send_command("addaccesscode")`, which is `device.py:45-51` POST `https://api.allegion.yonomi.cloud/v1/devices/{id}/commands` (`auth.py:25,115-128`);
   - only `accesscodeId` is read from the response (:290-294);
   - then a separate notification save (:297-308).
6. `auth.py:50-66` only checks the HTTP status (`raise_for_status`). The response body is never checked for a device acknowledgement.

**SET guarantee: REMOTE_SERVICE_ACCEPTED.** Success means the Allegion cloud returned 2xx for the command POST and for the notification save. No layer waits for or reports anything from the lock. Whether the cloud replies only after the lock acks is UNVERIFIED (no source). Things that change the guarantee:
- An entity that goes unavailable between LCM's `get_codes` and `add_code` turns the call into NOT SENT, with a normal return.
- The "already exists" swallow means nothing was written for this slot.

**CLEAR path**
1. `schlage.py:442-467`: `get_codes`, find the tagged name, `delete_code`, return True.
2. `_base.py:2238`.
3. `service.py:726/770` (same silent skip).
4. HA `lock.py:136-163`: re-fetch codes. A missing name returns success (:139-154). Otherwise `codes[id].delete`, then refresh.
5. pyschlage `code.py:310-329`: `send_command("deleteaccesscode")` POST to the cloud, then `notification.delete()`.

**CLEAR guarantee: REMOTE_SERVICE_ACCEPTED**, with the same conditions.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
1. **The "already exists" swallow** (`schlage.py:416`). HA's two duplicate errors render as English text (`exceptions.py:60-85`, `translation.py:217,433-458`):
   - `schlage_name_exists`: "A PIN code with the name … already exists"
   - `schlage_code_exists`: "A PIN code with this value already exists" (`strings.json:74-82`)

   Both contain "already exists", so both return `CONFIRMED`:
   - **Name exists:** the slot keeps whatever PIN the cloud already had. That can be the old PIN if the pre-delete failed, because its errors are suppressed (:403-406).
   - **PIN value exists under another name:** nothing is written for this slot, and its previous code was already deleted at :403-406.
2. **Silent skip when the entity is unavailable** (`service.py:726,765-770` plus `entity.py:48-52`). This can only hit inside the race window, because the first `get_codes` (which requests a response) raises instead. HA's own `delete_code` triggers a coordinator refresh (`lock.py:163`), and that refresh could flip `connected` before the add runs.
3. **Cloud accepted, lock never got it:** pyschlage issue #375 (https://github.com/dknowles2/pyschlage/issues/375) reports a code "visible in app … never reaches the lock". It was closed after the reporter found they were running an outdated install, so it is weak evidence.
4. **The reverse (failure that was actually applied):**
   - pyschlage #282 (maintainer comment, 2025-12-31): deleting a code created by pyschlage fails in the notification delete. That happens after `deleteaccesscode` has already been sent (`code.py:321-323`), so HA raises "Failed to delete" even though the cloud deleted the code.
   - Timeouts are not translated by pyschlage. `requests.RequestException(IOError)` (requests 2.34.2, `exceptions.py:20`) becomes `LockDisconnected`, even though the POST may have landed.

**Immediate read after a successful write:** it is a fresh request every time. `get_codes` → `lock.py:165-176` → `refresh_access_codes` → GET `devices/{id}/storage/accesscode` on the cloud (pyschlage `lock.py:330-375`). So the read is the cloud's record, never the lock. A read that agrees therefore doesn't prove the lock has the code, and a read that disagrees says nothing about the lock either.

Can it show the pre-write state? Only LCM's own comments say the cloud API is eventually consistent (`schlage.py:399-401, 411-415`). I found no upstream source for that. UNVERIFIED.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED classification match?**
- The `credentials.py:247-252` wording ("cloud service's word only") is accurate. `_base.py:1455-1462` records the write as pending with `believed=False`, which is appropriate.
- **Over-claim 1:** the "already exists" swallow returns `CONFIRMED` for a write that never happened.
- **Over-claim 2:** confirmation is by presence only. Occupied slots are always reported unreadable (`schlage.py:351-358`), and `coordinator.py:229-235` confirms on presence. So in the name-exists case the old PIN is confirmed as the new one.
- **Discrepancy:** LCM's docstring (`schlage.py:14-17`) says reads are masked. pyschlage builds `code` from the actual `accessCode` digits (`code.py:239`), and #282 shows a real read returning `code='2541'`. Whether the cloud ever masks codes is UNVERIFIED.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- Allegion cloud → lock delivery: the mechanism, its timing, and whether a 2xx waits for a lock ack.
- Cloud read-after-write consistency.
- Whether reads are masked.
- Lock firmware behavior.

## Akuvox

**Provider:** akuvox, domain `local_akuvox`. LCM's README:99 and wiki `Akuvox-integration.md:15` (@930de6d6) link pjaudiomv/hass-local-akuvox, which returns HTTP 404. I traced tykeal/homeassistant-local-akuvox @ 15d3d84d instead. Its domain, service names and schemas match LCM's calls exactly (`services.py:91-123, 226-282`). It requires `pylocal-akuvox&gt;=1.6.0`, which I read at v1.6.0 @ 9e3e691a.

**SET path**
1. `providers/akuvox.py:374-404`: `list_users`, then `modify_user(id, name, pin)` if the slot's user exists, otherwise `add_user`. Always returns `CONFIRMED`.
2. `_base.py:2238`, then `service.py:726/770` (unavailable entities silently skipped). Entity availability is defined at integration `lock.py:412-418`.
3. Integration `lock.py:1258-1305` (`add_user`) and `1307-1367` (`modify_user`). `modify_user` first re-lists users to verify the user is local (`_fetch_local_user`, :1159-1204). It catches `AkuvoxError` and raises `HomeAssistantError`; the unsupported-capability handler also raises (:454-466).
4. pylocal `_device_users.py:27-88` (capability gate) → `users.py`:
   - `add_user` (:68-151) POSTs `/api/user/set` with `{"action":"add"}`;
   - `modify_user` (:293-392) pages through GET `/api/user/get` (:191-209), merges the record, and POSTs `action:"set"` with the full record.
5. `_http.py:131-151, 273-303`: one request at a time (lock), 0.25 s delay after each.
   - HTTP 4xx or 5xx raises.
   - A non-negative `retcode` is success. Mutations normally return `retcode: 1` (PR #14 https://github.com/tykeal/pylocal-akuvox/pull/14; `specs/001-akuvox-http-library/research.md:17-20` @9e3e691a).

**SET guarantee: DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY.** Success means the device's own local HTTP API returned a non-negative `retcode`. That is the device answering directly (LAN only, no cloud), but there is no read-back after the write.
- Whether `retcode &gt;= 0` means the change is stored and enforced at the keypad is UNVERIFIED.
- pylocal PR #86 (https://github.com/tykeal/pylocal-akuvox/pull/86) notes that `/api/` uses a data store separate from the web UI. It says this only for groups and contacts. Whether it applies to users is UNVERIFIED.
- The race-window silent skip (item 1 below) turns this into NOT SENT.

**CLEAR path**
1. `akuvox.py:414-435`: `list_users`, then `delete_user(id)`.
2. `_base.py:2238`, then `service.py:726/770`.
3. Integration `lock.py:1369-1406`: `_fetch_local_user`; a missing user raises.
4. pylocal `users.py:395-401` POSTs `/api/user/set` with `action:"del"`.
5. `_http.py:273-303` applies the same `retcode` rule.

**CLEAR guarantee: DEVICE_RECEIVED_ONLY**, with the same conditions.

**Success-but-not-applied cases found**
1. **Silent skip when the entity is unavailable** (`service.py:726,765-770`). This only hits in the window between LCM's `list_users` (which requests a response and raises) and the mutation. Akuvox does not override `async_is_device_available`, so LCM's probe always returns True (`_base.py:1154-1156`).
2. **A non-negative `retcode` is treated as success.** Whether firmware ever returns a non-negative code for a rejected write is UNVERIFIED.

No other cases found. The reverse also exists: a timeout becomes `AkuvoxConnectionError` (`_http.py:187-189`), then `HomeAssistantError`, then `LockOperationFailed`, even though the POST may have been applied.

**Immediate read after a successful write:** `list_users` → integration `lock.py:872-893` → `device.list_users` → GET `/api/user/get` (`users.py:154-188`). It reads the same device store the write went to, with no cache in HA or the library. I found no source for asynchronous apply, so a stale pre-write read is not established either way (UNVERIFIED).

One concrete risk: the device page size is fixed at 10 (`research.md:105-106`). LCM never passes `page` (`akuvox.py:106-112`), but the library's own by-id lookup pages explicitly. If an unpaged GET returns only page 1, a user stored beyond the first 10 reads as absent right after a successful add. That is an incomplete read, not a stale one. UNVERIFIED. Open integration issue #128 (`delete_user` "not found" on an R20k) is unexplained.

**Does LCM's CONFIRMED classification match?**
- The handling is right: the write is recorded pending until a read confirms it.
- The `credentials.py:249-250` wording "the cloud service's word" is wrong for Akuvox. The word comes from the device's local API, so the docstring under-claims.
- Decision-relevant: Akuvox PINs are readable. In `coordinator.py:229-233`, a present slot with a different PIN marks the write failed immediately, with no grace period. So a modify followed by a read showing the old PIN counts as a contradiction at once. Nothing found shows that read could be stale.

**UNVERIFIED layers**
- Firmware semantics of `retcode` (persisted or enforced versus just accepted).
- Whether `/api/` users are the keypad's credential store.
- Whether an unpaged GET returns all users.
- Whether SmartPlus cloud sync can overwrite local users.
- Whether pjaudiomv's integration (the one LCM documents) behaves the same as tykeal's.

---

**For the decision:** neither provider guarantees the device applied the write when the call returns. Schlage gives the cloud's word, and its reads also come from the cloud. Akuvox gives the device API's acknowledgement with no read-back. So a disagreeing read can't be dismissed as stale on either.

The Schlage "already exists" swallow (`schlage.py:416`) is a real way to return `CONFIRMED` without writing anything. It matches HA's duplicate-PIN error as well as the duplicate-name one, and in that case the slot's code has already been deleted.

Nothing was edited. I ran `git fetch` for origin and upstream dev in the HA checkout, and downloaded source copies into `/tmp/pyschlage_src`, `/tmp/akuvox_ha` and `/tmp/pyakuvox`.</result>

## 2. Relevant

**#247 | Lock Code Manager is blocking Home Assistant from finishing startup** — fixed by PR #250, which did two things:
- **(a) Entry setup waits until Home Assistant has started.** Today that's `__init__.py:987-1000`. The spec doesn't touch it. **PRESERVED.**
- **(b) "Don't decide set/clear until we have accurate data from the lock."** PR #250 gated this on `coordinator.last_update_success`. Today the gate is `_resolve_credential_snapshot` returning None when `not self._coordinator.has_credential(...)` (`domain/sync.py:470`), so a slot with no coordinator data is skipped. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE.**
  - The spec's §5 says that with no informative observation, only belief speaks. It doesn't say whether "the lock has never been read this run" is still gated separately from "a read came back with nothing".
  - Scenario: a fresh install or a lost Store, and the lock's first read raises. Read literally, §5 gives belief ≠ fingerprint(P) for every active slot and belief ≠ "cleared" for every inactive one. Every managed slot is then written or cleared before LCM has heard from the lock. On an unreachable lock those writes raise and charge the slot breaker, which suspends a slot after three. That is the behavior #250 removed.

**#405 | Integration issues ("spamming locks", "clears and sets lock at start")** — no fix PR; it was closed by the 0.6.0 redesign. **PRESERVED, and improved.**
- Today `_last_set_pin` is in memory only (`sync.py:229-234`), and so is `last_write_was_clear`.
- So after every restart, an unreadable or write-only lock is judged out of sync (`calculate_in_sync`, `sync.py:540` and `:557`) and every slot is rewritten.
- The persisted belief in §4 removes that restart rewrite.

**#74 | Switch from binary sensor to sensor** — PR #82 split the sync state into two binary sensors, active and in_sync. A reporter on the issue asked that "PIN synced" go off as soon as the PIN changes and come back on once it is written.
- Under §5 that still holds: a PIN change makes belief ≠ fingerprint(new), so the sensor goes off; when the write returns, belief matches and it goes on. **PRESERVED.**
- **CHANGED-INTENTIONALLY:** the user-visible `sync_status` attribute value `pending_confirmation` goes away. It is defined at `domain/models.py:45`, exposed at `binary_sensor.py:190`, and rendered as "Confirming" at `ts/slot-card.ts:966-973`.
  - On a readable lock, in_sync now turns on as soon as the write returns. Before, it waited for a read to confirm.
  - The spec deletes the state but doesn't mention the card's switch case or the ordering at `sync.py:1150`. Those cleanups need to be in the plan.

**#241 | Not working after install, restart, and setup** — PR #251 made the lock-in-use checks ignore entries that aren't loaded. The code has since split this into two checks:
- `_lock_still_owned_by_another_entry` (`__init__.py:1062`, loaded entries only) is used for live teardown.
- `_lock_managed_by_other_entry` (`__init__.py:1012`, any configured, non-disabled entry, loaded or not) is used for permanent cleanup: `remove_permanently` at `:1104-1110` and `async_remove_entry` at `:1246`.

**NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** §4 says belief is removed "for a lock when it leaves its last entry" but doesn't name which check decides that.
- Scenario: the loaded-only check gets used. A lock is removed from entry A while entry B, which also manages it, is between loads. B's belief and `answered` flag are deleted. The cost is one rewrite per slot plus a re-armed §9 gap.
- Caveat: `_lock_managed_by_other_entry` passes `include_disabled=False`, so a disabled entry B also loses its belief under the persistent check, not only the loaded-only one.
- The spec should say "same check as `remove_permanently`".

**#61 | Ability to remove a device** — PR #65 added cleanup when a lock is removed from an entry. That is the same lock-removal path in the update listener where §4 adds belief removal. **PRESERVED** (the spec adds to that path, doesn't change it).

**#1121 | Reveal button toggles all codes** — closed as by design. The reveal state is card-wide (`ts/lock-codes-card.ts:199-210`, `:690-702`, `:744`). **PRESERVED**: §10 requires a sibling for every `SLOT_CODE_UNREADABLE` branch, and those include `_canReveal` (`:730`) and the mask class (`:744-751`).
- Small gap: §10 says the disabled state gets its own sentinel but not whether revealing shows a disabled Z-Wave slot's PIN, which is readable. It needs one sentence in the spec.

**#1320 | Upgrading (Z-Wave, "upgrading from 3.3.0 to 4.0.0 disabled half of my lock codes")** — no fix PR and no logs. The reporter said it went away after a later update. There's nothing traceable to preserve.
- "Disabled" is ambiguous here: it could mean disabled on the lock or suspended by LCM.
- If it meant disabled on the lock, §5's "disabled always contradicts" rule plus the Z-Wave clear-then-set rule would now repair those slots. Today they collapse into "holds a code" (`zwave_js.py:891`).

**#1583 | Withdrawn** (originally "6.0.0 config flow hangs after successful Matter user enumeration and may clear existing PIN users") — the flow hung at the allocation read ("Reading which slots your locks already hold") on an Aqara U200 over Matter. The author withdrew it as not caused by LCM. No fix PR, nothing established. One point bears on §8:
- Allocation skips Matter's index checks (`credential_index_follows_slot` is False, `allocation.py:134`, `_base.py:1791`).
- If §8's new `async_internal_read_indices` path keeps that skip, the spec's allocation changes don't apply to Matter.

## 3. Open feature requests

**#830 (RFID fobs on slots)** — not blocked, but complicated.
- §8 helps: a fob slot that reads as holding a code is "taken", so allocation never overwrites a fob.
- §7 already raises the unmanaged-code repair for it, as today.
- The complication is the §4 belief schema: `{slot}: "pin#…" | "cleared"`, keyed by slot alone and fingerprinted by `mask_pin`.
  - Coordinator data is already keyed by credential address (type + user ref), not slot alone (`coordinator.py:411-431`).
  - Managing RFID, or any second credential type, on the same slot would need a Store version 2 migration.
  - Keying belief by address with a credential-type field now would avoid that migration.
- There's also a gap on locks that have never answered a read. If a fob slot reads as "nothing", §8 treats it as free, so a fob there can be overwritten. The repair is the only disclosure.

**#858 (alarm panel codes)** — not blocked.
- Belief is keyed by entity registry id, which works for any entity domain, and the §1/§5 model doesn't depend on locks.
- What blocks it today is unchanged: providers and config are built around the lock domain.
- Same caveat as #830: if a panel's codes aren't plain PINs, the PIN-only fingerprint would need extending.</result>

<result>## Spec regression check: reader/virtual/display/events/conditions batch

Nothing in this batch regresses outright. The reader/codeless family is safe. Two gaps could bring back the #59 symptom or cause visible display regressions if the spec isn't made explicit, and the spec as written doesn't settle either one. I checked against main at `a2c2157f`.

| 18 | Fake lock for testing | direct | #103 (`cace6e89`): `VirtualLock` keeps slot state in its own `Store` (`providers/virtual.py:94-112`) and returns `CONFIRMED` (`:141`). Reads return every requested slot as known or empty (`:165-187`), so it never produces `nothing`. | §1, §3, §4, §9 | PRESERVED. Virtual pushes nothing itself, so the §3 removal list doesn't touch it. Its first read is informative, so the answered flag is set correctly. Belief and the virtual Store don't conflict: the virtual Store is only saved on unload (`:112`) while belief is saved on every write. After a crash, virtual reads empty, which contradicts belief and triggers one rewrite. That rewrite is not charged to the breaker ("wrote in this run"). This is correct self-healing. |

A `disabled` reading that still carries a PIN would otherwise render as an ordinary masked code instead of "Disabled on lock". |

| 1004 | Adding a condition after setup | indirect | #1005 (`1ad9d819`): replaced `mwc-*` components with `ha-*` in the condition dialog (a UI-only fix). | §10 | PRESERVED as a UI fix. §10 adds no form components. Its re-evaluation half is the same as #59. |
| 1213 | "No slot coordinator" warnings | indirect | #1214 (`c492cde1`): slot coordinators are created before per-lock entity setup (`__init__.py:~2151`). | §4 | PRESERVED. Advisory: the spec doesn't say when the shared belief Store loads. If it loads lazily on the `_async_setup_new_locks` path, a first tick can run before it is loaded and rewrite every slot. That would break the spec's own "no rewrite after restart" test, so the test would catch it. |
| 1520 | Deleted user leaves a ghost card | indirect | #1524 (`12b08804`): `_send_update` re-checks `has_slot` and sends `removed` (`websocket.py:1143-1152`). | §10 | PRESERVED. |

## Findings that need a spec rule, most severe first

**1. A silent lock never gets cleared when its condition turns off (#59 class, high).** The spec doesn't say whether `nothing` is a present entry in coordinator data or simply no entry. If it's no entry, three existing readiness gates stop sync entirely:
- `sync.py:466`: `if not self._coordinator.has_credential(...)` → skip.
- `sensor.py:96-101`: the code sensor is unavailable without a credential.
- `sync.py:439-442` and `:453-456`: sync waits while the code sensor is unavailable, and also while it is unknown and the slot is active.

Scenario: a Zigbee2MQTT lock that never replies, with a user gated by a calendar condition. The event ends, so the slot goes inactive. The code sensor is unavailable, so sync never runs and never writes the clear. The PIN keeps working outside the schedule, which is exactly the #59 symptom. The active path is blocked the same way.

Rule the spec needs: `nothing` is stored as an explicit value, so `has_credential` is true and the code sensor stays available with a defined state. Alternatively, the sync gates key on desired state plus belief, not the code sensor. Add an end-to-end test: a condition turning off on a silent lock issues the clear.

**2. After a set on a polled lock, nothing reads it back (#1493 and display, medium).** §6 deletes `record_write`, the only thing that triggers an immediate read after a returned set on a polled provider (`_base.py:1446-1449`). Clears keep their read-back refresh (`_base.py:1580-1581`), which the spec doesn't remove, so sets and clears become asymmetric. §10 picks the display row from the "latest observation", which can be older than the write.

Scenario: virtual lock (polled every minute, `_base.py:590-592`), managed slot. `set_credential` returns. The slot card shows "Synced" (belief), but the lock-codes card shows "—" from the pre-write `empty` for up to 60 s. The same happens when a code is written to an unmanaged slot through the card: it stays blank until the next poll.

Rule the spec needs: after any returned write on a polled provider, request one ordinary refresh. That is a genuine read, stamped after the write, so it isn't a self-push. And/or §10 judges display only on observations that arrived after the last write, falling back to the in-sync proxy otherwise.

Minor: belief written by unmanaged-slot writes (`websocket.py:1246`, `:1272`) has no removal trigger, because §4 removes records only when "no entry manages it any more". Harmless, but it leaks records.

**3. A trust-based "Synced" on the slot card is undisclosed (#1490, low).** §5 accepts "on is trust" for write-only locks, but §10 only changes the lock-codes card. `ts/slot-card.ts:943-994` `_renderLockRow` will show a green "Synced" with nothing marking it as trust, which is the exact harm #1490 described. The `pending_confirmation` case (`:966`) also becomes dead code once `SyncState.PENDING_CONFIRMATION` is deleted. Extend §10's "lock didn't answer" disclosure to the slot card's lock row, or state that it is deliberately unchanged.

**4. Cached data may be stamped as a fresh observation (outside this batch, low).** When the poll can't get the lock's turn (`LockBusy`), `coordinator.py:731-736` returns the cached `self.data` without calling `_apply_read`. If §2's arrival-time stamping is applied to what `_async_update_data` returns, that pre-write cache can be stamped after a write. A slot that should read "holds P" then shows a contradiction, gets charged, and is rewritten. Rule: re-returned cache is not an observation and must keep its original arrival time. The trigger is a narrow race; I didn't reproduce it, so this is UNVERIFIED.

## Your specific questions on virtual/codeless

- **Read as `nothing`:** no. `async_get_users` returns every requested slot (`virtual.py:165-187`).
- **Answered flag:** set correctly on the first read.
- **Double-store:** harmless. Belief holds only fingerprints, and when the two disagree after a crash, the observation wins with one uncharged rewrite.
- **Self-push removal:** doesn't apply; virtual never pushed its own writes.
- **Property 5 wording:** "LCM's writes never show up as observations" is literally false for virtual, whose genuine reads reflect what LCM stored. The property test needs to model virtual's Store as the device.
- **Write strength:** the spec doesn't say what strength virtual reports. That only affects diagnostics.</result>

## REGRESSES and NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, most severe first

1. **REGRESSES (#1335 class; also ZHA without events): removing self-pushes leaves stale data that other code still acts on.**
   - **What happens:** after a write on Zigbee2MQTT, the coordinator keeps the observation from *before* the write. Republishes of the stale cache are gated (`zigbee2mqtt.py:312-326`), and nothing re-reads until the hourly drift check (`_mqtt.py:89`). The spec filters by write time only inside the §5 in-sync rule. `_check_duplicate_code` (`_base.py:563-582`) reads `coordinator.credentials_by_slot()` with no such filter.
   - **Scenario:** slot 1 holds 1111 and slot 2 is to get 1111. Sync clears slot 1 (no self-push, so the data still says slot 1 = 1111). Sync then sets slot 2 = 1111. That raises `DuplicateCodeError`, and `sync.py:939-945` disables slot 2.
   - **On main:** the self-push at `zigbee2mqtt.py:578` empties slot 1 first, so this works.
   - **Escape:** a `pin_code_deleted` action triggers a refresh (`:246-258`), but whether a given lock emits it is UNVERIFIED, and the refresh is asynchronous.
   - **Display:** §10 would also show the old PIN for a slot that is in sync.
   - **Rule needed:** every consumer of observed data (duplicate check, display, websocket, allocation) must treat an observation older than the slot's last successful write as superseded, using belief or `nothing` instead.

2. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1307/#1397/#1549): cached Z-Wave JS reads serve pre-write state after an ambiguous write, and §2 doesn't list it.**
   - **Mechanism:** after an `ERROR_UNKNOWN` write, `zwave_js.py:666-670` deliberately skips the reconcile read, relying on the confirmation read. The spec deletes that read. On the HC620 the driver's verification read timed out, so its cache keeps slot 5 as Available.
   - **What follows:** every later cached projection (`zwave_js.py:287-288`, run by setup `_base.py:1093`, reconnect `_base.py:1301`, and restart) delivers `empty` after the write. §5 treats that as a contradiction, rewrites, and charges if the write was in this run.
   - **Compared with main:** the scoped confirmation read repaired that cache about 94% of the time. Under the spec it never does.
   - **Rule needed:** Z-Wave JS delivers `nothing` for a slot whose cached value hasn't been updated by a device report since LCM's last write to it. Alternatively, keep an integrity-checked single-slot read after an ambiguous write.

3. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1335): the Zigbee2MQTT integrity fix isn't specified, and the obvious version undoes #1336.**
   - **Why it's hard:** this converter answers GetPinCode only through the `users` republish, which looks exactly like a cached republish.
   - **Risk:** if §2's "can't establish integrity, deliver `nothing`" is applied to every read answered from `users`, the lock reads as silent. The "answered" flag (§9) is never set, and allocation (§8) then treats unanswered slots as free on a lock that holds keypad codes.
   - **Rule needed:** distrust slot N only while LCM has written N since N's entry last changed, and deliver `nothing` for that slot only.
   - **Also:** state explicitly that the delta gate stays. Its written justification (protecting the optimistic push) disappears with §3, which invites someone to delete it.

4. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1307 mechanism, PR #1437): a write that raised doesn't invalidate belief.**
   - **Current rule:** §4 writes belief only on success, so a set that raised but landed (verification timeout) leaves belief at "cleared".
   - **Scenario:** a silent or withholding lock has belief "cleared". A set of P raises but lands. The user disables the slot before the retry. The latest observation is `nothing`, belief is "cleared", so the slot reads in sync while P still opens the door.
   - **On main:** `_base.py:1389-1393` discards the cleared marker before the write, so this case gets re-cleared.
   - **Rule needed:** invalidate the slot's belief before issuing a write, or when the write raises.

5. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1106): the reconcile read can't be dropped as the §2 fix without a minimum driver of 15.25.2.** Otherwise every cached read on older drivers serves pre-write state, with the same consequence as finding 2.

The spec is at `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-08-credential-model-design.md`. Line numbers are against main at a2c2157f.</result>

Seven issues (#199, #209, #289, #358, #818, #1397, #1523) are "closed" without a closing reference. Five have no fix PR at all. For #1397 I used #1410, #1408 and #1550, and for #1523 I used #1525.
