| 1552 | Stopping a sync manager waits out the operation deadline | indirect | PR #1562: bounded stop grace, then cancel (sync.py:316-395, STOP_GRACE_SECONDS const.py:253). | §4, §6 | **PRESERVED.** A cancelled write never updates belief, so it is retried. Removing the confirmation task simplifies shutdown (coordinator.py:808-819). |

**REGRESSES / NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, most severe first**

1. **HIGH: the breaker never trips, so the #776/#848 infinite loop comes back.** `request_sync_check` sets `_breaker_reset_requested` on every IN_SYNC → OUT_OF_SYNC transition (sync.py:701-704), not only when the target changes. The next tick resets the breaker before anything is charged (sync.py:794-796). Today a write sits in PENDING_CONFIRMATION, so a contradiction never passes through IN_SYNC. Under §5 every returned write is IN_SYNC at once. So: write → IN_SYNC → contradicting report → reset → charge 1 → rewrite, repeated forever, draining the battery. §5 lists `:900` and `:1058` but not `:703`. **Rule:** that reset may fire only when (active, PIN) differs from the last-synced target.

2. **HIGH: a disabled user's PIN keeps working.** Today `_cleared_slots` is discarded *before* a set (_base.py:1388-1393) because "a set that raises may still have landed". §4 updates belief only on success, so `"cleared"` survives such a set. Scenario on a lock that withholds values or is write-only:
   - Belief is `cleared`; the user enables the slot with P.
   - The set raises (a #1525 timeout, or the Z-Wave verification read timing out) but P actually landed.
   - The slot is then disabled (by the user, or across the profile by `async_disable_slot`).
   - Desired is cleared, belief is `cleared`, and no observation exists, so the slot reads in sync. P still opens the door, and the Store makes this survive restarts.
   
   **Rule:** remove the slot's belief before issuing any write, and set it only on return. The same rule covers delete-then-add providers (Matter, Schlage) whose belief stays at P_old after a partial write.

3. **MEDIUM-HIGH: false duplicate-code rejection disables a slot.** `_check_duplicate_code` (_base.py:563-582) scans coordinator data. Under §3 that data no longer holds LCM's writes. Example: slot 1 changes 1234→5678 and the data still shows 1234. Slot 2 is then set to 1234 → DuplicateCodeError → slot 2 is disabled across the whole profile (sync.py:939-951). It also misses real duplicates against LCM-written PINs on locks that withhold values. Belief can't stand in, because `mask_pin` mixes the slot number into the hash (util.py:76-92), so hashes can't be compared across slots. **Rule:** ignore observations older than the other slot's last write. Compare against other managed slots' configured PINs where belief == fingerprint(configured).

4. **MEDIUM: sync can stall in LOADING on write-only locks.** §10 covers only the card. The Python code sensor's `native_value` (sensor.py:79-92) needs mappings for `nothing` and `disabled`. If `nothing` maps to None (state unknown), `_ensure_entities_ready` blocks every active slot (sync.py:453-456). Sync then never leaves LOADING on write-only locks.

5. **MEDIUM: a dormant path that charges on silence goes live.** With the pending path deleted (sync.py:1028-1031), every write to a polling lock reaches the post-write `async_refresh`. Its failure branch charges the breaker (sync.py:1045-1050). Three failed read-backs then suspend a slot whose write returned: the #1397 failure mode on polling providers. **Rule:** delete that charge, because a failed read is silence.

6. **MEDIUM: Z-Wave JS cache stays stale on a flaky link (#1397).** If the post-write reconcile read fails, the driver's cache keeps the pre-write state for every later cached read, including after a restart (zwave_js.py:955-958). The spec deletes #1550's scoped fresh confirmation read, which was what repaired it. While the lock is unreachable, recovery polls run at 60 s and longer intervals and read the cache. Each returns a stale `empty`, which counts as a contradiction and charges the breaker, so three can suspend the slot. §4's "after a restart … nothing older needs ignoring" is false for this cache and for retained MQTT topics, which §2 itself lists. **Rule:** extend §2: a cached read that can't be shown to postdate the slot's last write delivers `nothing`.

7. **LOW:** the #64 display lag (row above), the #1061 breaker-routing wording, and the #225 "not read yet" gate.

8. **One more spec problem, outside this batch:** §8 adds `lock_does_not_report_codes`, keyed on the entity registry id, to `PER_LOCK_ISSUE_KEYS`. But `async_remove_entry` builds those issue ids from the entity id (`__init__.py:1246-1251`, `per_lock_issue_id` util.py:71-73). The new repair would never be cleaned up when an entry is removed.</result>

### Findings, most severe first

1. **REGRESSES (#1491/#443 class): the new repair's cleanup can never find it.** §8 keys `lock_does_not_report_codes` on the entity registry id and relies on `PER_LOCK_ISSUE_KEYS` for cleanup. But `async_remove_entry` builds each id as `per_lock_issue_id(key, lock_entity_id)` = `f"{key}_{lock_entity_id}"` (util.py:71-73, __init__.py:1248-1251), which never matches a registry-id key. Separately, `_async_release_locks` deletes only `lock_dropped` (:1162-1164). So when the options flow or reauth removes a write-only lock from its last entry, the belief record goes but the repair stays forever: its other exit, the answered flag, can't be set once the lock is unmanaged and its record is gone. **Rule:** build the id from the registry id at every cleanup site, and delete this repair in `_async_release_locks` (checking only whether another entry names the lock) as well as in `async_remove_entry`.

2. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, HIGH (#1581): where allocation gets the "answered" flag is undefined.** Allocation always reads through its own temporary provider instance, never the shared one or its coordinator (allocation.py:224-267).
   - **Scenario:** a new entry is created for a flaky (#1397-class) lock with a keypad code in slot 1. Slot 1's reply is dropped (`nothing`) and slot 2 answers empty. There is no Store record yet, so the flag is unset, slot 1 is freed, and the user's PIN overwrites the keypad code. Today slot 1 counts as occupied and is avoided.
   - **Rule:** any informative answer from a lock anywhere in the current allocation (across all widening passes) counts as "answered" for that allocation. Allocation reads the persisted flag via `lock_entry.id`, which `build_lock_instance` already resolves (allocation.py:91).
   - **Adjacent:** `delete_user` with `clear_credentials=false` deliberately leaves a code on the lock (`retained_pairs`, __init__.py:2107-2124). §4 then drops that slot's belief, and on a never-answered lock allocation frees the slot, so the next user overwrites a code LCM was told to keep.

3. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, HIGH (#1491/#1348): belief records can be orphaned permanently.**
   - **Wrong function named.** §4 puts removal in "the update listener's lock-removal path". It must go in `_async_release_locks`, or reauth skips it, which is exactly #1491.
   - **Failed-setup reauth skips removal.** The lock entity is deleted (the #1348 trigger), setup fails, and reauth swaps the lock out. `_async_release_locks` returns early at :1155, so no removal runs.
   - **The record can't be found afterwards.** The entity is gone, so its registry id can't be resolved from the entity id. `async_remove_entry` can't find it either, and the record (with PIN fingerprints) plus its repair stay forever.
   - **In-flight writes can re-create it.** On a loaded entry, if removal runs before `async_unload_lock` (:1168), a tick finishing inside `STOP_GRACE_SECONDS` (sync.py:316-370) writes the record back.
   - **Rule:**
     - Each record carries its lock entity id.
     - Remove records before the early return when the entry never loaded, and after `async_unload_lock` when it did.
     - Refuse belief writes for a lock that no entry names.
     - When the Store loads, prune records that don't resolve to a lock in any entry, and delete their repairs.

4. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, medium (#443): abandoning the setup flow orphans the repair.** The UI setup flow allocates at the count step (config_flow.py:545-554), before the user enters any user details. Abandoning the flow leaves the repair (and any flag record the temporary instance wrote) for a lock in no entry, and nothing ever deletes it. **Rule:** only raise the repair or persist a record for a lock that is in an existing entry; the load-time prune in finding 3 also covers this. Delete the Store file once it is empty.

5. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, medium (#865/#1476): one Store object, owned by the integration.** Two lock instances per lock are normal, so the Store must not live on a lock instance or an entry (row 865).

6. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, low (#1535): keep today's exception handling.** `LockBusy` charges nothing and `LockDisconnected` feeds the lock breaker; "Raised = failed" must not override that (row 1535).

7. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, low (#1476): disabled sibling entries.** Removing a lock's record when a disabled entry still names the lock loses that entry's belief and the answered flag (row 1476).

8. **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE, low (#48/#70): slots of a deleted entry on a shared lock.** `async_remove_entry` should also remove the deleted entry's slots from records of locks that stay in other entries (row 48). The spec's tests need a two-entry shared-lock case for the Store and repair (row 70).</result>

**REGRESSES and NEEDS-SPEC-RULE findings, most severe first**

1. **#1257, REGRESSES:** the §2 "0x85 is a definitive rejection" fix turns the U200's startup `unknown(133)` back into an immediate slot disable (`sync.py:939-951`).
2. **#1083, REGRESSES:** Schlage's lagging cloud reads lose the 60 s pending window and `last_write_was_clear`. Combined with the immediate read-back (`sync.py:1037`, `_base.py:1581`) and delete-then-add, this produces churn and then suspension. §2 doesn't list Schlage read integrity.
3. **#1538 (b), NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** a Matter `occupied_disabled` user is projected as present, so it shows in sync while the PIN doesn't work, and a rewrite keeps it disabled.
4. **#1538 (a), NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** a Matter clear event is stamped after an async read, so an echo of LCM's own clear can land after the next set and cause a false contradiction plus a charge. Stamp at receipt and reconcile against the fresh read.
5. **#1268, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** the `LockBusy` branch re-publishes cached data (`coordinator.py:729-733`). It must keep the original arrival stamps, or pre-write observations get judged as post-write.
6. **#1579, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** §8's `contents=None` conflicts with Matter's deliberate not-read `None`. Keep the skip and the gate explicit (`allocation.py:259-275`, `occupancy.py:37,61`).
7. **#1518, minor NEEDS-SPEC-RULE:** name the `pairs_removed` loop (`__init__.py:2108`) as where slot belief is removed, whatever the release outcome.

**Outside this batch:** #1437 (6b4702e4) added `last_write_was_clear` for Z-Wave masked reads and Zigbee2MQTT "enabled with no PIN". Under §5's cleared branch, "holds a code" after a clear returns out of sync, so #1437's endless re-clear now ends in suspension rather than in sync. Whoever has #1437 should check it.</result>

<result>## Matter batch regression review (PRs #1044–#1289)

Three findings need spec rules. The worst: the spec's new requirement to pass `user_status=occupied_enabled` on Matter's update path runs into the update-path tolerance added by #1245/#1246, so a failed re-enable gets swallowed. No PR in the batch is regressed outright, and none of these findings repeat the prior review.

| PR | Title | Relevance | Behavior established (current file:line) | Spec § | Verdict | Scenario + needed rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| #1044 | Matter duplicate PIN on restart | direct | A `sync` duplicate on a credential LCM owns is cleared and retried with a fresh index. A second duplicate, a `direct` source, or a duplicate on a credential LCM doesn't own raises `DuplicateCodeError` (`matter.py:1006-1091`). `source` is threaded through `_base.py:1354-1427`. | §3.3, §4, §7 | PRESERVED (rule needed, F2/F4) | Belief now avoids the restart rewrite. The path still runs on a belief-loss rewrite and on a disabled-slot rewrite. F2/F4 cover its clear-then-raise side effect. |
| #1045 | Auto-detect service response | none | Replaced by #1077, which removed the service calls | — | irrelevant | — |
| #1046 | Pass user_index to set_lock_user | indirect | `set_lock_user` gets keyword `user_index` and `user_name` (`matter.py:750-755`) | §5 Matter trap | PRESERVED | `user_status` is a valid parameter (`lock_helpers.py:346`). See F1 for the KeyError trap. |

An up-transition refreshes and resubscribes (`_base.py:1270-1306`). | §3.4, §10 | PRESERVED | A failed §3.4 read "charges nothing", which matches today's confirmation look. ## Findings, most severe first

**F1 — HIGH, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1245/#1246 against §5's Matter re-enable rule).**
- **Where it breaks:** §5 puts the #1538b fix on `set_lock_user`'s update path. That path deliberately swallows every failure (`matter.py:620-639`) on the premise that "only the cosmetic name update is lost". Once it carries `user_status`, a lock that rejects SetUser (the #1245 production `UnknownError … InvalidCommand (0x85)`, or a #1246 charset rejection on all three names) silently keeps the user `occupied_disabled`.
- **Scenario:**
  1. The credential write still returns, and belief is set.
  2. The §3.4 read sees `disabled`, which is charged as a contradiction.
  3. The rewrite repeats. On locks that answer a same-PIN modify with `duplicate`, that is #1044's clear-and-re-add each time.
  4. After 3 tries the slot is suspended with a misleading reason.
- **If the §3.4 read is silent instead:** belief alone reads in sync while the PIN doesn't work. That is the #1538b false in-sync again, and it lasts up to an hour until the drift check.
- **The opposite fix (making UPDATE raise) is also wrong:** it brings back #1245's spurious suspensions.
- **Rule needed:**
  - Pass `user_status` on every name candidate.
  - If every name fails with `MatterError`, issue one status-only modify (`user_name=None` keeps the lock's existing name; see `lock_helpers.py:427-431`) with `user_status="occupied_enabled"`.
  - Only the name may be tolerated. A status write that fails raises `LockOperationFailed`.
  - List this tolerance under §2 as a kept, name-only exception, so nobody deletes it as an "honest writes" violation.
- **Side note:** the modify path indexes `USER_STATUS_REVERSE_MAP[user_status]` directly (`lock_helpers.py:439`). A wrong string raises a `KeyError` that escapes every except clause (`matter.py:620`, `:756-774`) and lands in the sync catchall that suspends the slot (`sync.py:1000-1018`). Pin the exact string with a test through the real helper, which is #1046's lesson.

**F2 — MEDIUM, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (§3.1's #1523 claim isn't delivered on Matter; touches #1044 and #1049).**
- **Mechanism:** §2/§5 only advance "last write" on a successful completion. A raised write invalidates belief but supersedes no observation.
- **Scenario:**
  1. A new Matter slot has a pre-write observation of `empty`.
  2. `set_lock_credential` times out after the device applied it, which raises `LockDisconnected` (`matter.py:940-948`).
  3. The slot is then disabled.
  4. §5 finds `obs is empty` and returns True, so no clear is issued and the PIN keeps working.
- **Why it lasts on Matter:** nothing re-reads it. There is no poll (`coordinator.py:108`), LCM writes emit no events (`matter.py:992-994`), and the next read is the hourly drift check (#1049). Today has the same hole, so this is not a regression, but the spec says §3.1 fixes it, and the spec's #1523 end-to-end test would fail on Matter.
- **Rule needed:** a raised write records its issue time as a supersession point for every consumer. It sets no belief and no completion time.

**F3 — MEDIUM, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1049 drift check as Matter's only periodic read).**
- **Equality gate:** the drift check publishes only `if new_data != self.data` (`coordinator.py:792`); the same gate is at `:296` and `:507`. If stamps are stored outside the compared values, a fresh read that equals the pre-write values is dropped. Example: after a clear, the lock still reports "holds a code" (Q3). The old stamp then makes the contradiction superseded, and sync reads in sync on belief "cleared". Rule needed: a fresh observation's stamp is news even when its value is unchanged, and no publication gate may drop it.
- **Dropped event — UNVERIFIED:** `matter.py:1545-1548` drops LockUserChange events with lockDataType UserIndex and operation Modify, on the grounds that the event "says nothing about its PIN". I'm assuming an out-of-band status change arrives as exactly that event, per the Matter spec; I didn't verify that in the repo. Under §1's `occupied_disabled` → `disabled`, that justification no longer holds. Until the event triggers a re-read of that user, detection waits for the hourly drift check, which is also skipped while the lock breaker is tripped (`coordinator.py:765-773`).

**F4 — LOW, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1044 path; what does "no change" do?).**
- **Scenario:**
  1. #1044's sync path clears the existing credential (`matter.py:1043-1049`).
  2. The retry raises `DuplicateCodeError`, and the slot is disabled.
  3. The lock now holds nothing, but the pre-write "holds a code" isn't superseded, so §5 issues a clear.
  4. `async_delete_credential` finds no PIN and returns False (`matter.py:1211-1213`).
- **The gap:** §3 says "Returned: set belief and completion time", but it doesn't say whether a "no change" result counts. Today's code deliberately treats a no-op clear as no evidence (`_base.py:1574-1579`). If the spec follows suit, this slot re-issues the clear every tick, uncharged, until the hourly drift check.
- **Rule needed:** state whether "no change" sets belief and the completion time, and whether it triggers the §3.4 read. My recommendation is all three, since the provider's lookup was itself a fresh read.

**Not reported (pre-existing, not caused by the spec):** a PIN swap between two Matter slots makes the lock itself return `duplicate`. §7's LCM-side check doesn't cover that, so one slot is disabled, the same as today.</result>

<result>The revised spec still breaks or leaves unstated six behaviors from past fix PRs in this batch: two regressions and four rules it needs to add. Most of the batch is preserved. This was read-only against main at a2c2157f, and I left out anything already in the prior review.

## Findings, most severe first

**1. HIGH, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#737, also hits the #848 case). A same-value re-observation is dropped, so a silently refused write reads as in sync.**
- `push_update` returns early when the new data equals the old (`coordinator.py:567-569`). The Z-Wave report path `_confirm_slot`→`observe_push` (`_base.py:542-552`, `coordinator.py:357`) lands there, and so does the confirmation read when nothing changed (`:507`).
- Today, a refused write still triggers a re-check through three things:
  - the self-push (`known(P)`) changes the data first;
  - otherwise `_failed_writes` plus `async_update_listeners()` fires (`coordinator.py:358-360`, `:509-513`).
- The spec removes all three. Scenario: slot 2 is empty, LCM writes P, and the lock silently refuses it (a duplicate, or a "sent"-only provider). The post-write read says empty, the same value as before, so it is dropped. The stored "empty" keeps its pre-write stamp and is superseded, so belief decides. The slot reads in sync forever and nothing is charged.
- **Rule:** every fresh observation refreshes its stamp and notifies listeners even when the value is unchanged. Only re-published data keeps its old stamp.

**2. HIGH, REGRESSES as worded (#1243). §4 removes a lock's belief record "after `async_unload_lock` on a loaded entry".**
- `async_unload_lock` also runs from `async_unload_entry` (`__init__.py:1207`) on every HA shutdown, reload and disable. #1243 moved persistent cleanup out of unload for exactly this reason.
- Read literally, every restart wipes belief and the `answered` flag. That brings back the restart rewrites (#405). It also clears a flaky lock's flag, so §9 frees its silent slots again (#1581/#1397).
- **Rule:** remove a lock's record only on the permanent-release path (`_async_release_locks`→`async_unload_lock(remove_permanently=True)`, `:1168`). Apply the same downgrade it uses when another entry still manages the lock (`:1107-1111`). Never remove it in `async_unload_entry`. The same applies to deleting `lock_does_not_report_codes`.

**3. HIGH/MED, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#737). After the post-write read, the tick must decide the state itself.**
- `request_sync_check` ignores coordinator updates while the slot is SYNCING (`sync.py:699-737`). Today the tick re-checks after its own read (`:1054-1067`), which is what #737's guard relies on.
- §5 says "every returned write is in sync at once", and §3.4 treats the read as "an ordinary observation". If the tick just sets IN_SYNC, a contradiction that arrived during SYNCING is lost until some later data change.
- §6 deletes `take_failed_write`, the one-shot latch that made "charged once" true. The spec doesn't say where the contradiction charge now happens: the breaker may only be changed inside the tick (`:156-162`), and it must be once per write.
- **Rule:** after step 4, the tick evaluates §5 against current observations and charges any contradiction there, at most once per write.

**4. MED, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1050, #987, #1346). The breaker-reset rule leaves sites and mechanisms undefined.**
- "Reset only when an informative observation agrees or the target changes" doesn't address these existing resets:
  - `sync.py:617` (`_disable_slot`, #987);
  - `:629` (`_suspend_slot`);
  - `:724` (resume from suspension when in sync);
  - `:734` (resume after the lock is reachable again, #1346/#532).
- Once `:703` is deleted, the only target-change detector left is `:719-723`, which runs only while SUSPENDED. A target change while OUT_OF_SYNC or IN_SYNC resets nothing. #1050's case (stale counts, then a PIN change to a duplicate) depends on that reset.
- If the rule compares against a stored target, disabling and re-enabling with the same PIN looks like no change. Today `:617` covers that.
- "Agrees" isn't defined for "holds a code". On Matter, where #1050 was found, a successful write would then never clear earlier `LockOperationFailed` charges, while `:1058` does today.
- A consistency note: the agreeing post-write read resets the breaker on every cycle of a slow keypad fight. So the spec's test "keypad fight trips after three (#776)" only holds for fights that overwrite before that read. That isn't a regression, since `:1058` resets today too.

**5. MED, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#987, #1243). Repairs get cleared on trust.**
- `_clear_resolved_issues` runs whenever the slot reaches IN_SYNC (`:902`, `:1060`). With in-sync-at-once, a persistent `slot_suspended` or `slot_disabled` repair is deleted the moment a write returns, before the post-write read contradicts it.
- Example: a slot was suspended before a restart (the suspension target lives only in memory). After restart it rewrites, the repair is deleted on return, and then the read contradicts.
- Today, polled providers clear it only after a read-back.
- **Rule:** clear these repairs on the same evidence that resets the breaker (an agreeing observation), or on a returned write only for locks that have never answered.

**6. MED, REGRESSES (#1453 path). The `retained` belief contradicts the `pairs_removed` removal rule.**
- Retained pairs are part of `diff.pairs_removed` (`__init__.py:2108-2122`). §4 removes "a removed user's slots, whatever the release outcome" in that same loop, which deletes the `retained` marker §9 relies on. On a lock that has never answered, allocation then frees the slot and overwrites a code the caller asked LCM to keep.
- §9 also doesn't say which wins between "empty → free" and "retained → taken". If `retained` wins, it never expires, and every add-then-remove cycle burns a slot, which is #1453's second symptom.
- **Rule:**
  - The loop writes `retained` for retained pairs and removes records only for released ones.
  - An informative "empty" observation drops `retained`.

**7. LOW, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#1453). Does the release write record belief?**
- The release clear goes through `async_internal_clear_usercode` (`_base.py:1963`), so steps 1-2 of §3 would run. If it records belief after the `pairs_removed` removal, it leaves a per-slot record that the load-time prune never removes, because that prune works per lock.
- **Rule:** either name release writes under §3.6 ("record no belief"), or order the removal after the release.

**8. LOW, NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (#928). Keep the Store object when its file is deleted.**
- "Delete the file when it is empty" must not drop the `Store` object from `hass.data[DOMAIN]`. `async_setup` (`__init__.py:594-597`) won't run again when a new entry is added later.</result>

The configured-PIN stand-in only when managed and in sync; eye hidden otherwise (`ts/lock-codes-card.ts:717-731`); hollow dots sized to known length (`:772-779`) | §12 | **NEEDS-SPEC-RULE** (low). If the lock's registry row is gone or renamed, building the registry-id-keyed record and repair id fails, and both survive until the next Store-load prune. The Store-load prune is the state-based analogue. Leftover per-slot beliefs are benign: belief is read only for managed slots, plus `retained` in allocation. |
| #1515: subentry migration review | Every `SlotAllocationError` key has a sentence in each allocating flow, checked by a test that reads keys from `allocation.py`. An unnumbered user's edit allocates or refuses cleanly | §9 | PRESERVED. The "refused like a failed read" case reuses `occupancy_unknown`, and any new key is caught by the guard. |
| #1516: staged options settle; repair counted twice | Options settle on both exits; reconfigure allocates `len(others)+1` | §4, §9 | PRESERVED. Belief lives in a Store, not entry options; the allocation count is untouched. |
| #602: entity creation blocking | Entities never wait on external readiness | §4, §11 | PRESERVED. Loading the Store in `async_setup` is a local file read; §11 gives defined sensor states. |
| #926: resource cleanup broken by extra key in `hass.data` | Cleanup now keys on whether other entries are loaded (`__init__.py:1213-1223`), not the shape of `hass.data[DOMAIN]` | §4 | PRESERVED. Adding the Store under `hass.data[DOMAIN]` is safe. |
| #1203: per-lock PIN column removed | The legacy `use_slot_cards: false` layout still renders code sensors | §11 | PRESERVED; follows from §11's sensor states. |
| #1117: card a11y / pending label | "Pending sync" label is driven by the pending class (`:480`, `:688-697`) | §12 | Covered by the #1282 rule (a). |
| #850: no URLs in translation strings | URLs go through `description_placeholders` | §9 repair | PRESERVED. The repair text has no URL; when the wiki link is added, use a translation placeholder. |

## 3. Findings, most severe first

1. **#1482, unload vs release (§4 removal table, row 2).** "After `async_unload_lock` on a loaded entry" is ambiguous. `async_unload_lock` also runs on every entry unload (`__init__.py:1207`): reload, disable, and the reload reauth triggers (`config_flow.py:793`). If removal is hooked there, every reload wipes belief and the "answered" flag:
   - Matter locks rewrite every slot by delete-then-add.
   - A flaky lock (#1397) loses "answered", so the next allocation frees a silent occupied slot and replaces a keypad code. It also raises a false `lock_does_not_report_codes` repair.

   **Rule:** records are removed only when a lock leaves an entry (inside `_async_release_locks`), when a user is removed, or when an entry is deleted. A plain unload never removes them.
2. **#1482, disabled sibling.** §4 names `_lock_managed_by_other_entry` for the check, but that function scans with `include_disabled=False` (`__init__.py:1028-1031`). That contradicts the next clause, "must not drop records belonging to a disabled sibling entry". A disabled sibling therefore loses its slot beliefs and the lock's "answered" flag. **Rule:** use a presence check that includes disabled entries, both here and in the Store-load prune ("a lock in any entry").
3. **#1519, cleartext PIN for disabled slots.** The readable PIN shown for a "disabled" slot must follow `reveal` masking in `_slot_code_payload`.
4. **#1482, releasing a shared lock.** Rows 1 and 2 remove "the lock's record". With the presence gate, the record is kept whole, so the departing entry's slot beliefs linger. **Rule:** release removes the releasing entry's `pin:{slot}` entries, and removes the whole record, flag and repair only when no entry (including disabled ones) names the lock. This mirrors the `async_remove_entry` row.
5. **#1282 / #1117, card.** `lockHasCode` at `lock-codes-card.ts:480` is not enumerated; derive pending from `in_sync`. Add a contract test pinning `SlotCode` to `SLOT_CODE_*`.
6. **#1442, cleanup when the lock's registry row is gone.** Cleanup sites can't build the record or repair id from the registry, so they need a fallback that matches the stored `entity_id`.

**Outside the PR list, but contradicts the spec's own test:** the setup flow allocates with `config_entry=None` (`config_flow.py:303-307`, `:405-414`). Under §9's "only for a lock in an existing entry", a brand-new entry on a silent lock never gets the repair. The spec's end-to-end test ("setup ... the repair is raised") then fails unless the repair is also raised at first setup or first sync.

## Regressions and missing rules, most severe first

**1. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (HIGH, #817/#1334): the ERROR_UNKNOWN rule in §2 can't be implemented as written.** The spec calls Z-Wave ERROR_UNKNOWN-as-success a violation "even when it carries an explicit rejection". LCM has no way to tell which case it is:
- **Driver:** UC `setCredential` returns the same `Error_Unknown` for "the verification GET didn't confirm" and for "supervised Fail, and the read-back doesn't match" (`AccessControl.ts` ~1183-1226, upstream/master).
- **HA:** `lock_helpers.py:121` turns both into the single key `credential_rejected_unknown`.
- **Consequence:** making it raise would raise on every write to a write-only or masked Z-Wave lock, which is what the provider docstring (`zwave_js.py:631-643`) says this result means. Belief is invalidated each time, three charges suspend a slot whose PIN works, and #1397 comes back.
- **Rule:** ERROR_UNKNOWN stays a returned write with strength `sent`, and §3.4/§5 decide.
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether a verification-GET timeout returns undefined (giving `Error_Unknown`) or throws.

**2. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (HIGH, #1327/#1471/#819): Disabled is never classified on the Z-Wave push paths or on zwave-js-ui, so "Disabled always contradicts" isn't enforced.**
- **Z-Wave JS shim:** `_uc_slot_in_use` returns `status == ENABLED` (`zwave_js_server/util/lock.py:109-113`), which makes Disabled look the same as Available. A Disabled slot's `userCode` push becomes `known(PIN)`, `known("****")` or `empty` (`zwave_js.py:912-941`).
- **Z-Wave JS read path:** the base projection ignores `User.active`, although the driver maps Disabled to `active=false` (`AccessControl.ts:1620`).
- **zwave-js-ui poll:** a Disabled slot comes back `unreadable` (`zwave_js_ui.py:292-323`), i.e. "holds a code", which is in sync on belief.
- **zwave-js-ui push:** a Disabled status pushes nothing. Its retained `userCode` can arrive before the status (the `True` default) and push `known(PIN)`.
- **Consequence:** a disabled slot holding LCM's PIN reads in sync indefinitely, and §5's clear-then-set trap never fires.
- **Rule:** classify from userIdStatus first (Disabled → `disabled`, carrying the PIN if readable, #1121) on all four paths. Note that zwave-js-ui writes pass `ENABLED` explicitly (`:1299`), so it doesn't need clear-then-set.

**3. REGRESSES (MED-HIGH, #819, already broken on main since #1327): a masked code on an Available slot becomes `known("****")` (`zwave_js.py:932-937`).**
- **Driver behavior:** it persists status before code (`UserCodeCC.ts persistUserCode`), so the code event sees `in_use` False.
- **Scenario:** a slot desired cleared, on a lock that reports Available plus `****`.
- **Consequence under §5:** the masked value is treated as holding a PIN, which contradicts belief "cleared" and is charged. After three, the slot is suspended with a false repair saying the lock still holds a code. #776 is the same alternating-asterisks loop.
- **Rule:** a status of Available means `empty` whatever the code field says.

**4. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (MED-HIGH, #1344 scenario, #1327 principle): the Z-Wave read reports slots it knows nothing about as `empty`.**
- **Mechanism:** the driver returns no credential when a UC slot's status is missing from its cache (`AccessControl.ts #getCredentialCached_UC`). The base projection then fills it with `empty()` (`_base.py:1621`).
- **Consequence:** on a lock interviewed while asleep, or a write-only lock, every slot reads "empty":
  - §10's "answered" flag gets set.
  - §9 frees slots that may hold keypad codes.
  - The `lock_does_not_report_codes` repair is never raised.
  - Every restart causes a rewrite.
- **Rule:** a missing cache status is `nothing`, not `empty`. #1327 already applies this principle on the push side ("zeros from a partially-loaded cache must not be misread as cleared").

**5. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (MED, #1334): §2 keeps the reconcile read and says it "becomes" §3.4's post-write read, but doesn't say where it runs.**
- §3.4 runs only after writes that returned, but #1334 also reads before raising a definitive rejection and after a failed delete (Defect 3). Those must stay in the provider.
- On the success path, the read currently runs inside the write call. Anything it produces arrives before the write's completion time and is discarded as superseded (§2/§7). Running §3.4's read as well means two device GETs per write, on the #1397 links where each failed GET holds the lock's turn for the full timeout.
- **Rule:** the success-path read moves to §3.4. Raise-path reads stay in the provider. Both count as "refreshed from the device" for §2's cache rule.

**6. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (MED, partly UNVERIFIED, #1327/#819): the stale-AVAILABLE guard that §2 keeps only covers the status event (`zwave_js.py:889-909`).**
- The `userCode` event from the same report (`""` or zeros, with `in_use` False) still pushes `empty` (`:914-923`, `:934-935`).
- Today that causes an uncharged rewrite. Under §5 it is a charged contradiction. If #863-class locks send this report after every set, slots get suspended.
- zwave-js-ui's guard does cover the whole report, because a blank code never pushes.
- The spec should define the guard per report, and say whether it applies to §3.4's immediate read. That read is unguarded, and #863's lock sends a stale AVAILABLE about 3 s after a set.
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether those locks' paired `userCode` value changes.

**7. NEEDS-SPEC-RULE (LOW, UNVERIFIED ordering, #1471): §2 only distrusts *retained* zwave-js-ui topics.**
- A *live* value-topic publish can be the driver repeating back LCM's own write (it persists the value after a supervised success). If that publish arrives after the API response, it is taken as the lock agreeing.
- The per-slot "written since last refresh" marks also must not be reset on teardown or resubscribe, which is how `_slot_enabled` behaves today (`:715`, `:1388`).

## Outside my batch, but serious enough to flag (HIGH)

§5 resets the breaker whenever an informative observation agrees. §3.4 puts a fresh read after every returned write. In a keypad fight, or any loop where LCM's rewrite actually lands (the #776 alternating-asterisks shape), every cycle goes:
1. The contradiction is charged (count 1).
2. LCM rewrites.
3. The §3.4 read agrees, and the count resets to 0.

The breaker can never reach three, so the spec's own test ("A keypad fight trips the breaker after three") can't pass. The spec needs to decide whether an agreeing read that directly follows LCM's own write may reset the breaker.</result>

<result>I've written the PR A plan and saved it to `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-pr-a-provider-honesty.md`. Before writing it, I implemented every task in a scratch copy of main @ a2c2157f (`/private/tmp/claude-501/pra-scratch/repo`). There the full suite passed (2474 passed, 3 skipped), coverage stayed at 100%, and `prek` was clean. I ran each new test once against the unfixed code, and each failed as the plan says. Nothing in the main checkout was touched apart from the new plan file.

**Tasks:**
- **0:** Create the worktree `.worktrees/provider-honesty` on `fix/provider-honesty` from origin/main, reinstall `requirements_dev.txt`, and record the baseline (2450 passed, 3 skipped on a2c2157f). origin/main is 13 commits ahead, but they are dependency bumps only and touch none of these files.
- **1: zwave-js-ui reads the Supervision result.**
  - 255 (Success) → `CONFIRMED`.
  - 2 (Fail) or 0 (NoSupport) → `LockOperationFailed`.
  - 1 (Working), missing, or a boolean → `OPTIMISTIC`.
  - A new `BaseLock._request_read_back` handles the clear side: a clear the lock didn't confirm returns `True`, isn't pushed empty, and schedules a coordinator read. Without that, a push provider would keep showing the old PIN, and sync would keep re-clearing without ever counting a failure.
- **2: ZHA, a set/clear reply with no status.** A set returns `OPTIMISTIC`; a clear is read back.
- **3: ZHA, reply `user_id` must match the slot.** A mismatched reply, or one with no `user_id`, is handled like the timeout path: `unreadable()`. Real zigpy replies always carry `user_id`, so the 16 hand-built test fakes that lacked it are converted to the real zigpy reply schema by a scripted step.
- **4: Z-Wave JS, Available decides first.** Fixes #819: an Available slot with a masked code no longer reads as `known("****")`.
- **5: Z-Wave JS, one stale-AVAILABLE guard for both halves of a report (#863).** The end-to-end test runs on the real coordinator.
- **6: Matter.** The `LockUserChange` user read now goes through `_execute_rate_limited` and also catches `LockBusy`.
- **7:** Full suite with coverage, `prek run --all-files`, a code-review pass, push, and the PR from the template (no Breaking Change section).

**Deferred, with reasons:**
- **zwave-js-ui `UserCodeCCGet` userId matching can't be checked from LCM.** In node-zwave-js (`packages/cc/src/cc/UserCodeCC.ts`), `get` returns only status and code, dropping `userId` (`:573`). `UserCodeCCGet` is also declared to accept any User Code Report from the node, with no check on which slot it names (`:1636-1638`). It needs an upstream node-zwave-js change.
- **Matter `_dispatch_lock_operation` (`matter.py:1447`)** has the same outside-the-turn read but was left out. It only fires keypad-use events, and queuing it behind a sync walk could delay or drop them. You need to decide whether it should take the turn too.

**Decision needed before merge:** the spec-mandated #863 change means that while LCM expects a PIN on a User Code CC slot, a real keypad deletion is also ignored. Z-Wave JS has no periodic hard refresh, so nothing catches the deletion until some other read happens. Today the code half of the report happens to let that deletion through. This is flagged in the PR body and the reviewer notes.

**Spec claims found wrong or imprecise:**
- **Guard exemption for LCM's own reads** (spec §2 guard bullet): the spec says the guard doesn't apply to LCM's post-write GET. On today's code the reconcile reads (`zwave_js.py:681`, `:698`, `:722`) arrive through the same value events with nothing marking them as LCM's own, so PR A can't honour that exemption. The effect is bounded and documented; the exemption belongs with §3.4 in PR E.
- **ZHA matching** (spec §2 integrity table): the spec says replies are matched "by sequence number only". zigpy actually matches on endpoint, cluster, direction and sequence number (`zigpy/device.py:639-654`). The conclusion still holds, since it never checks the user.

- **Task 4:** full suite, `prek run --all-files`, push, PR from the template (no Breaking Change section).

**Design decisions:**
- **Where the check runs:** the target comparison is right after the snapshot in the tick. The breaker is only read or charged there, so this covers every state and keeps the tick as its only mutator.
- **First target:** the first target seen is adopted without a reset.
- **Accepted edge case:** a write the coordinator gives up just before a target change (within one 2-second tick) can be charged once to the new target. That is at most one failure, aged out in 5 minutes.

**Deferred to E:**
- **Repairs clearing only on evidence:** today, in-sync rests on belief for masked slots (`sync.py:536`, `:552`) and for push providers' own pushed writes. Telling those apart from real lock reports needs E's model, and the "lock has answered" flag from B. C doesn't change when `_clear_resolved_issues` runs.
- **The charge on a failed post-write refresh (`:1045-1050`):** deleting it alone would let a polled slot whose verification read keeps failing retry forever.
- **The once-per-write contradiction charge:** needs E's post-write read.

**The surviving mutant from the earlier draft:** removing the resume-from-code-suspension reset is an equivalent mutant, not a gap in the property or its inputs. `_suspend_slot` already requests a reset (`:629`), and `_async_tick` returns before the tick body while the slot is suspended (`:770-775`), so that request is still pending when the slot resumes. I kept the `:724` reset as the spec says. The property does catch removing target detection, restoring `:703` or `:900`, and removing `:734`.

**Spec claim found wrong:** spec §5 (design spec lines 355-357) says target-change detection today runs "only while SUSPENDED (`:719-723`)". While IN_SYNC, the `:703` reset already catches it indirectly; that was #1050's fix. What's actually missing is detection while OUT_OF_SYNC, PENDING_CONFIRMATION or SYNCING, where `request_sync_check` does nothing. That means #1050's bug still exists on main in those states, and Task 2's third test fails on main because of it.

**Is a contradiction after a confirmed write ever charged today?** No, so the roadmap's claim holds.
- **Confirmation removes the pending record:** confirming a write deletes it (`coordinator.py:226-235` for reads, `:349-351` for pushes).
- **Push providers never record one:** their confirmed writes skip the pending record entirely (`_base.py:1439-1444`).
- **So the contradiction isn't charged:** a later contradiction goes straight into coordinator data, nothing is flagged as a failed write, and the tick just rewrites. Only the rewrite's own failure can be charged.
- **The narrow exceptions are all before confirmation:** a contradiction that displaces a write still pending is charged once (`coordinator.py:230-232`). So is one seen by the immediate post-sync refresh of a write that recorded nothing pending (`sync.py:1062-1065`).</result>

**Decisions the plan makes that you should know about:**
- **"Refuse writes" is checked per lock and slot, not per lock.** A write is recorded only if some entry manages that slot on that lock (`find_entry_for_lock_slot`). That one check also keeps out unmanaged-slot writes, release writes, and a late write landing after its lock has left every entry.
- **Release forgets after teardown.** `_async_release_locks` forgets belief in a `finally` that runs after the teardown, so an entry that never loaded still forgets before returning. It now takes the slot numbers explicitly, because the options-flow listener has already replaced the config by the time it runs.
- **Tests that build a provider directly** now request a new `belief_store` fixture, which calls the same loader `async_setup` uses. The `lock_code_manager_config_entry` fixture teardown now tolerates an entry the test deleted.

**Deferrals:**
- **`answered` flag, `retained` value, and repair cleanup on whole-record removal and prune:** PR F. The repair doesn't exist yet, and leaving these out now means no migration later.
- **Completion times and supersession:** PR E.
- **Matter-specific restart test:** the change doesn't depend on the provider, and the shared write path and sync manager are tested with `MockLCMLock.write_only`. It fits better with PR E/F's restart tests, where the `answered` flag also has to survive.
- **"Unload never removes belief"** is a test run once for each of the three unload kinds rather than a Hypothesis property. With only three events that is exhaustive, and Hypothesis with function-scoped HA fixtures is a known trap here.
- **No timing test for "forget after teardown":** the order is guaranteed by the `finally` in code, not by a test.

**Spec claims found wrong or in tension:**
1. **Regression review line 61 contradicts the spec.** It says "still managed by another entry" uses `_lock_managed_by_other_entry`, but that helper skips disabled entries (`__init__.py:1028-1031`), and design §4 (around line 270) correctly calls it the wrong check. The plan follows §4.
2. **Design §3 step 2 ("Returned, including 'no change': set belief") is wrong for clears.** A no-change clear means the provider found nothing to clear. Today that deliberately does not count as cleared (`_base.py:1574-1579`), and `test_a_clear_that_changed_nothing_is_not_evidence` (`tests/test_binary_sensor.py:2246`) pins it. PR B keeps today's behavior for clears and records no-change sets; PR E should settle the spec line.

<output-file>/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-raman-projects-lock-code-manager/88689b8f-7a06-4f90-9808-9d17aef5d545/tasks/ae2808cc8ba503da2.output</output-file>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I re-reviewed commit 37612587. Important #2 is fixed and verified, and the NoSupport, comment and docstring items are done. Important #1 is only partly fixed: when a second unconfirmed clear lands within 10 s of a previous read, the lock gets re-cleared until a deferred read runs, and that deferred read is outside the tracked task. It's a one-line fix, and I'd make it before Task 2.

### Strengths
- **Read-back moved onto the coordinator, as recommended.**
  - `request_read_back()` is tracked in `_read_back_task` and uses `eager_start=False`.
  - It does nothing once `_shutdown_requested` is set, and `async_shutdown` cancels it (`domain/coordinator.py:386-418`, `856-858`).
  - `BaseLock._request_read_back()` is now a thin wrapper with no arguments.
- **Cancellation works and nothing swallows it.** `_async_read_back` has only `try/finally`, and HA's `_async_refresh` catches only specific exception types, never `CancelledError`. `test_shutdown_cancels_a_read_back_in_flight` checks that the cancel reaches the inner read (`completed == []`).
- **No bad interaction with `_confirm_task`.** The confirmation look calls `async_internal_hard_refresh_codes` directly, so the two never share the debouncer. At worst they queue two walks of the lock, one after the other.
- **Important #2 is fixed, and I checked it.** With `self._request_read_back()` removed in a scratch copy, `TestUnconfirmedWrites::test_an_unconfirmed_clear_ends_in_sync_without_being_reissued` fails (`data[slot1]` stays `known('1234')`). It passes with the call in place.
- **The rest:**
  - NoSupport → OPTIMISTIC now matches what the driver does (`Driver.ts:7699-7711`).
  - "Command Class" is spelled out.
  - The history wording in the test docstrings is gone.
  - Both docstrings now state that the read goes through the ordinary read.

### Issues

#### Critical (Must Fix)
None.

#### Important (Should Fix)

**1. Important #1 is only partly resolved: the read-back still goes through HA's 10 s refresh debouncer.**
- **Where:** `domain/coordinator.py`, `_async_read_back`, which calls `await self.async_request_refresh()`.
- **What happens:** if any debounced refresh ran in the last 10 s (the default `REQUEST_REFRESH_DEFAULT_COOLDOWN`), `Debouncer.async_call` just marks a call due at the end of the cooldown and returns. The tracked task finishes at once, and the real read happens later in HA's own debouncer task.
- **Two consequences:**
  - **Redundant clears.** Until that read runs, the coordinator still shows the old code, and clears are not charged to the breaker, so the sync tick re-clears every 2 s.
  - **Cancellation gap.** The deferred read runs in HA's `_handle_timer_finish` task, not in `_read_back_task`. If it is already running when shutdown starts, `async_shutdown` does not cancel it. That is the same orphan-walk exposure #1 was meant to close. A deferred read that is still only waiting on its timer does get cancelled.
- **Reproduced** in a scratch copy at 37612587, with the unsupervised table, slot 1 disabled at T+0 and slot 2 at T+4, using a clock that accumulates:
  ```
  T+2s  clear[1] get[1] get[2]    in_sync [off, on]
  T+4s  -                         [on, on]
  T+6s  clear[2]                  [on, off]
  T+8s  clear[2]                  [on, off]
  T+10s clear[2] get[1] get[2]    [on, off]
  T+12s get[1] get[2]             [on, on]
  ```
  Slot 2 gets three clears and two extra walks of the lock. On a slow mesh with more managed slots, that grows.
- **Why the suite misses it:** the e2e helper `async_advance_time` (`tests/conftest.py:409-423`) fires `utcnow() + delta`, which doesn't accumulate. The debouncer's 10 s `loop.call_later` handle therefore never comes due. Using `async_advance_time` in the same scenario, slot 2 never settles at all: a clear every tick and no reads.
- **Fix:** in `_async_read_back`, call `await self.async_refresh()` instead of `async_request_refresh()`. The tracked task then owns the read (so cancelling it covers the walk), there is no cooldown deferral, and `_read_back_task` still coalesces requests.
- **Fix verified in scratch:** with that change, the same scenario gives exactly one clear and one walk per slot, each settling within one tick. The full suite passed with it (2468 plus my 3 scratch tests).
- **Regression test:** disable slot 1, then disable slot 2 two ticks later with the existing `async_advance_time`, and assert `clear[2]` is issued at most twice. It fails on 37612587 and passes with the fix.

**On whether coalescing can drop a needed read:** it can't today, and it still can't with the fix.
- A read in progress holds the lock's turn, so no clear can run in the middle of it.
- With `async_refresh()` there is no awaited inner task, and HA's `_async_refresh` has no await after `_async_update_data`. Once the read gives the turn back, the task runs straight through its `finally` and clears `_read_back_task` before a queued clear can finish its own exchange with the lock.
- A request that arrives after that starts a fresh task.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)
1. **The docstring overclaims.** "A read already in flight absorbs the request … a read-back never outlives the provider" is not true while the debouncer can defer the read. It becomes true once Important 1 is fixed.
2. **The cancel is not awaited in `async_shutdown`.** This matches how `_confirm_task` is handled, and the test shows the cancel lands, so it's fine as is. Base `async_unload` awaits its `_reconnect_task` cancel if you want the stricter pattern.
3. **The base docstring names ZHA early.** `_base.py` lists "(zwave-js-ui, ZHA)" as callers, but ZHA only starts using it in Task 2. That's fine if Task 2 lands in the same PR.

### Assessment
**Ready for Task 2 after one small fix.** Swap `async_request_refresh()` for `async_refresh()` in `_async_read_back` and add the two-slot regression test. Do it before Task 2, because ZHA will call the same helper.

## What changed, item by item

**Important 1 (suspension repair for a clear):**
- `sync.py`: when the slot breaker trips, the reason now depends on `snapshot.active_state`. An active slot keeps the old wording. An inactive slot now says the slot should hold no code but the lock still held it after N attempts to clear it, so that code may still open the door. It also says Lock Code Manager stopped retrying, and that sync resumes once the slot is active again or the code is removed at the lock.
- `strings.json` and `translations/en.json` (still identical): the repair description's fixed sentence "will resume automatically once the lock accepts the code or you change the PIN" contradicted the clear case, so I removed it. Each reason now states its own resume condition.
- The two other suspension reasons (lock will not accept it, unexpected error) depended on that sentence, so each gained "Sync resumes once this slot's PIN or active state changes."

**Minor 4 (unmanaged slots):** `_record_unconfirmed_clear` now records a pending clear only if `is_slot_managed(code_slot)`. This covers release, direct service and repair-flow clears. By the time the release path runs, the slot is already gone from the entry's config, so it counts as unmanaged.
- One nuance: the leak was narrower than described. `SlotSyncManager.async_start` already discards any failure recorded before the manager starts, so a reused slot number could only be charged inside a short window after start.
- That is why the new test checks the coordinator boundary directly (`has_pending_write` and `take_failed_write` both false for the unmanaged slot) rather than going through a reused slot. A reused-slot test would pass even with the bug.
- The test is `tests/providers/zha/test_e2e.py::test_an_ignored_clear_on_an_unmanaged_slot_leaves_nothing_to_charge`.

**Minor 1 (stale pending clear after a confirmed clear):** I made it exact. The base takes `started_at = time.monotonic()` before the provider call. Afterwards, `coordinator.drop_superseded_by_clear(address, started_at)` drops whatever is pending unless it is a clear recorded at or after `started_at`.
- I used a timestamp rather than snapshot identity because the snapshot would be taken before waiting for the lock's turn. A set recording itself during that wait would then be wrongly kept.
- Tested by `test_a_clear_drops_what_it_superseded_but_not_its_own_record` in `tests/test_coordinator.py`.

**Minor 2 (stronger property test):** the property test (`tests/properties/test_clear_charges.py`) gained these rules:
- `clear`: issues a clear without waiting for its read, so several clears can be pending at once.
- `push`: delivers a push through `_confirm_slot`, the real push-event path that calls `observe_push`. `_push_credential_update` bypasses `observe_push`, so I didn't use it.
- `set_code`: a confirmed set, which supersedes a pending clear.
- `settle`: fires the look timer and waits until everything is read.
- `charge`: takes each slot's verdict exactly once.

The machine's slots are now managed through a real Lock Code Manager entry. The oracle is that a slot owes its last clear's verdict, and nothing after a changed set.

**Minor 6 (naming and wording):** renamed `_request_read_back` to `_record_unconfirmed_clear`, updating ZHA, zwave-js-ui and the tests. The two provider unit tests now assert the provider calls `_record_unconfirmed_clear(slot)`. The warning now reads "(a code not seen by the deadline or replaced by another, or still holding a code after a clear)".

**Minor 7:** both zwave-js-ui convergence assertions are now `== 1`, and they pass.

**Minor 9:** changed to "a code this integration cleared".

## What holds

**The three Task 1 fixes still hold.** This commit doesn't touch the confirmation look, shutdown or `record_clear`'s shutdown check.
- Shutdown still cancels the look in flight and refuses new clear records.
- The tick gate still blocks re-clears while a clear is pending.
- The read-back still calls the provider directly, with no debouncer.
- The convergence tests are now `== 1` and pass.

**Important 1 (suspension wording) is fixed.**
- `strings.json` and `translations/en.json` are byte-identical (`cmp`).
- No "accepts the code" text is left in the template.
- Each reason reads correctly alone, and each matches the real resume rule (target change or back in sync):
  - set suspension: keeps the full original text;
  - clear suspension: says the code "may still open the door";
  - lock won't accept it: "Sync resumes once this slot's PIN or active state changes";
  - unexpected error: same sentence.
- Both e2e tests check the new clear wording.

**Minor 4 (unmanaged slot) is fixed, and you were right that the leak was narrower than I said.** `async_start` already discards failures recorded before the manager starts (`sync.py:297-299`).
- Release runs from the update listener after the config changes, so `is_slot_managed` is already false and nothing is recorded.
- Removing the managed-slot check is killed by the new ZHA e2e test.

**Minor 1 (timestamp) is sound.**
- `written_at &lt; started_at` keeps ties. A tie can only be this clear's own record, or a stale clear recorded during the turn wait, so ties fall on the safe side.
- On macOS and Linux, `time.monotonic` has nanosecond resolution, so ties only happen under frozen test time. The unit test covers that tie (slot 3).
- A set recorded during the turn wait is always dropped, because `pin is not None`. That is correct: the clear ran after it on the device. Records are written in the same synchronous step that releases the turn, so nothing can slip in between.
- No input causes a wrong drop. One case causes a stale keep, below.

**Minors 6, 7 and 9 are done.**

**Pyright is still a false positive.** At runtime the class order is `UnconfirmedClearLock → MockLCMPushLock → MockLCMLock → BaseLock`, and `UnconfirmedClearLock._record_unconfirmed_clear is BaseLock._record_unconfirmed_clear` is `True`.

**Mutation testing:** I ran 6 mutants against the full suite and all 6 were killed:

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|

| No managed-slot check | ZHA e2e |
| Timestamp taken after the call | 7 tests |

## Remaining issues

**1. Should fix: the property test's interleavings never happen.** File: `tests/properties/test_clear_charges.py`.
- The confirmation look finishes inside each `clear` rule's `run_until_complete`. I instrumented a scratch copy:
  - 0 of 2470 `clear` rules left anything pending;
  - 0 of 1903 `push` rules found a clear waiting.
- So these docstring claims never happen in a run:
  - "clears may pile up before anything reads them";
  - a push settling a waiting clear;
  - a set superseding a waiting clear.
- That is why the push mutant survives the property test.
- **Is the oracle independent?** Partly. The per-clear rule ("owes the last clear's verdict") is independent. But `push` decides whether a clear is still waiting by asking the implementation (`self.coordinator.has_pending_write`).
- **Fix:**
  - Gate the mock lock's `async_hard_refresh_codes` on an `asyncio.Event` that only `settle` and `charge` release, so clears really are left waiting.
  - Track "waiting" in the model: `clear` sets it, and a push, a changed set or `settle` clears it. Then drop the `has_pending_write` query from the push rule.
  - The push mutant should then die in the property test.

**2. Minor, optional: take the timestamp inside the turn.** File: `_base.py:1577`.
- `started_at` is taken before the turn wait. If another clear on the same slot records itself pending during that wait, and this clear is then confirmed, the stale record is kept.
- No wrong charge results, only up to 15 s (`CONFIRM_READ_INTERVAL`) in pending confirmation.
- Setting `started_at` in a `pre_execute` callback would make it exact. `_execute_rate_limited` already runs `pre_execute` inside the turn (`_base.py:377`), as the set path does.

**3. Minor, a cost of the Minor 4 fix.**
- An unconfirmed clear on an unmanaged slot now gets no read-back.
- On the release path, the old code for the released slot stays in the coordinator's data until the next full read: any other look, a programming-event refresh, or the hourly drift check. Until then the card's websocket feed and diagnostics show it.
- At 79c350fc it was read back right away.
- Since `async_start` already closes the leak, the check only closes a race of a few seconds: a slot re-added while the released slot's look is still in flight.
- **Alternative:** keep the record and the read-back, and skip only the failure flag for slots nothing manages.
- I worked this out from the code and did not run it.

**4. Nit:** after the "this integration" edit, the docstring paragraph at `coordinator.py:213-215` wasn't reflowed. Line 214 is 89 characters (E501 is ignored, so lint passes).</result>

## Item 1: the property test now reaches waiting clears
- **Where the gate sits:** I wrapped the machine's `coordinator.async_confirm_pending_writes` to wait on an `asyncio.Event` that only `settle` and `charge` open. I did not gate the lock's `async_hard_refresh_codes` as you suggested: that method runs while the look holds the lock's turn, so a held read would block every clear queued behind it and the next rule would never finish. This gate sits before the turn, still on the real look.
- **Model, not implementation:** the machine now tracks a `waiting` set. A clear adds its slot; a push to that slot, a set that changes it, or `settle` removes it. The push rule no longer asks `has_pending_write`.
- **Targeted rules:** I added `push_to_a_waiting_clear` and `set_over_a_waiting_clear`, which only run when something is waiting and pick a waiting slot.

| Rule | Runs | Hit a waiting clear |
|---|---|---|
| `clear` | 1909 | 1909 left something pending; 1035 found another clear already waiting |

| `set` | 1450 | 194 changed sets over a waiting clear (13%) |

- Push never fails a pending clear (`failed = False`): now killed. Falsifying example: `clear(kind='empty', slot=1)`, then `push_to_a_waiting_clear(kind='readable')`, then `charge()`, giving `False is True`.

- New test `test_a_clear_supersedes_a_clear_recorded_while_it_waited_for_its_turn`: an unconfirmed clear records itself while a second clear is queued for the turn; the second runs, is confirmed, and must leave nothing pending.

<result>**Approved.** Commit 4d35fd88 has no remaining issues. I checked `git diff 9d72f106..4d35fd88` in `/private/tmp/claude-501/review-scratch/t2b-r3/`. The worktree is unmodified: `git diff --quiet HEAD -- custom_components tests` exits 0, and HEAD is 4d35fd88. In scratch the full suite gives 2490 passed, 3 skipped, coverage 100%.

**Gate placement.** Holding the look before it takes the lock's turn is right, and better than what I suggested: holding it inside the turn would stall every clear queued behind it.
- The wrapper replaces `async_confirm_pending_writes` on the instance, so `_async_confirmation_look` finds it. Once released, the real look runs: the real lock read, `_apply_read`, the comprehension, `_fail_overdue`, and the timer handoff through `_settle`.
- While held, `_confirm_task` is still in flight, so later `record_clear` calls fold into one look. That is the coordinator's real coalescing behavior, not a bypass.
- One interleaving it can't produce is a clear queued behind a look that is already reading. That path is safe: the turn is released in `finally`, and the look finishes synchronously before the clear runs.
- I instrumented the model's `waiting` against `has_pending_write`, taken before each push or set. They agreed in all 2063 cases:

| Rule | Found a clear waiting | Total |
|---|---|---|
| push | 319 | 1796 |
| set | 259 | 1800 |

- Every clear left something pending (1 to 3 slots).
- **Oracle:** it is now independent. `waiting` is tracked in the model, and the `has_pending_write` query is gone.
- **Gap (no change needed):** a set over a waiting clear with the same code (the no-change path) never occurs. That case is benign.

**`chargeable` threading.** It is complete.
- `_apply_read` checks it (`coordinator.py:240`), and so does `observe_push` (`:404`).
- The look's comprehension and `_fail_overdue` never fail a clear at all, so they need no check.
- `PendingWrite.chargeable` defaults to `True`, which only sets use, and set paths ignore it.

**Unmanaged slot becoming managed, or the reverse.** `chargeable` is fixed when the clear is recorded. Neither direction causes a wrong charge.
- **Unmanaged, then managed before the read.** The record stays non-chargeable. A readable result is not charged, and the new manager's own clear is chargeable. If the slot is now active, the tick drops the clear as stale. At worst, one charge is skipped.
- **Managed, then unmanaged before the read.** A failure may be recorded with no manager to take it. `async_start` discards it if the slot comes back. The only gap is a read that finishes after a re-added manager starts, a window of seconds, as before.

**Timestamp.** It is now exact.
- `pre_execute` runs at the top of `_execute_locked` (`_base.py:377`), inside the turn. While the turn is held, nothing else records against the slot.
- `started[0]` can't raise: the only successful return from `_execute_rate_limited` goes through `_execute_locked`, and no test patches `_execute_rate_limited`.

**Mutation testing.** I ran 8 mutants against the property, coordinator, provider and sync tests. All 8 were killed:

| Mutant | Killed by |
|---|---|
| A push never fails a clear | property test and unit test |
| `record_clear` doesn't discard an earlier failure | property test |
| `_apply_read` ignores `chargeable` | unit test and ZHA e2e (ignored) |
| `observe_push` ignores `chargeable` | unit test |
| Always chargeable | ZHA e2e (ignored) |
| Never chargeable | property test and both suspension e2e tests |
| Timestamp taken before the turn wait | new turn-wait test |
| No read-back for an unmanaged slot | ZHA e2e (applied) |

**Task 1 fixes.** All three still hold.
- This commit doesn't touch shutdown, the look's task tracking or the tick gate.
- `record_clear` still checks `_shutdown_requested`.
- The read-back still calls the provider directly, with no debouncer.
- The convergence tests are still `== 1`.</result>

<result>**Approved.** Nothing blocks Task 7. Three small notes are optional and could go into this PR as a test-only follow-up.

The Matter suite passes on 1370a14e in scratch (196 tests). I also ran six mutations against `tests/providers/matter`:

| Mutation | Result |
|---|---|
| Remove the stale-clear rule entirely | killed |
| Remove `holds_pin` from the condition | killed (by `test_pin_cleared_pushes_empty`) |
| Remove `resolved.is_empty` from the condition | killed |
| Remove the `code_slot is not None` guard | **survives** (note 1) |
| Make `holds_pin` count any credential type | **survives** (note 2) |
| Remove the ADD/MODIFY early return | survives, as the coordinator said |

### 1. Can the stale-clear rule hide a legitimate clear?
- **A clear that genuinely failed on the device:** no. A `LockUserChange` CLEAR is the lock reporting that its user/credential data actually changed, so a clear that failed on the device produces no CLEAR event for the rule to skip. A CLEAR event plus a read showing a PIN therefore means a write came after it: the clear-then-set retry, an index reused by the next add, or a lock that reports a modify as clear-then-add. Skipping is right in all three, and the ADD event that follows pushes `unreadable`. LCM's own failed clear is caught in the write path, not here. Before Task 6 the code pushed `empty` here unconditionally, which was only ever correct by accident.
- **Remaining gap (rare, no change needed):** the read can still return stale data if the server merges it with a `GetUser` that `_dispatch_lock_operation` sent outside the turn before the clear. That needs a keypad-use event in the same window.
  - If LCM made the clear, nothing is lost: `async_delete_credential` already pushes `empty` (`matter.py:1227`).
  - If the clear came from outside LCM, it is now skipped and stays invisible until the hourly drift check. Before this commit it would have pushed `empty`.
  - It comes from the decision to leave `_dispatch_lock_operation` unchanged. Not worth handling now; noting it so it is known.
- **An untagged user:** behaves as before. With an untagged owner, `code_slot` is `None`, so the rule does not apply. The "present but untagged" branch drops the anchor, and the event is ignored.

### 2. `asyncio.Lock._waiters`
This is the first use of it in the repo. Tests elsewhere use `_aio_lock.locked()` and acquire/release, which are public `asyncio` API. `_waiters` is an internal attribute of CPython's `asyncio.Lock`: it is `None` before anyone waits and an empty deque once they have, and both read as false, so the polling works on 3.14. It is acceptable for a test.

A public alternative is to assign `matter_lock._aio_lock` an `asyncio.Lock` subclass whose `acquire` sets an `asyncio.Event` before calling `super().acquire()`, and wait on that event. Non-blocking.

### 3. The drop test
The weakness is acceptable. Without the early return, ADD/MODIFY still reaches the "did not resolve" branch and pushes nothing, so the early return only changes the log line. A test can only tell them apart through caplog, and the memory notes prefer avoiding caplog assertions. The test does still catch `LockBusy` escaping the task, via the harness error at teardown.

### Optional test gaps (Minor)
1. **The `code_slot is not None` guard is untested.** Without it, a CLEAR whose index now belongs to an untagged user holding a PIN returns before the anchor is dropped. A later CLEAR on that index would then wrongly resolve through the stale anchor. Suggested test:
   - Anchor 42 to slot 3, then send `CLEAR(42)` while the read shows an untagged user 42 with a PIN. Expect no push.
   - Send `CLEAR(42)` again with an empty read. Expect still no push.
2. **`holds_pin` ignoring non-PIN credentials is untested.** Fix: add `{"type": "rfid", "index": 1}` to the credentials of the remaining user in `test_pin_cleared_pushes_empty`. A user holding only RFID must still get `empty`.

The scratch export and mutation script are `/private/tmp/claude-501/review-scratch/t6-quality/r2/` and `/private/tmp/claude-501/review-scratch/t6-quality/mutate.py`. The script restores `matter.py` in a `finally`. Nothing in the repository was modified.</result>

**1. `code_slot is not None` guard.** The new test is `test_a_clear_for_an_index_now_held_by_an_untagged_user_drops_its_anchor`.
- **Setup:** it anchors index 42 to slot 3 through a normal read, then sends CLEAR(42) while the read shows an untagged user "Guest" at 42 holding a PIN.

I removed the now-unused `SimpleNamespace` import and the "or none" wording in the docstring.

**4. Public-API turn watching, done.** `_WatchedLock` is a small `asyncio.Lock` subclass with two events: `queued`, set on entry to `acquire`, and `abandoned`, set when a queued `acquire` is cancelled. `_hold_the_turn` installs it as `matter_lock._aio_lock`, starts the write, then clears `queued` so only later callers register. It now returns `(release, write, turn)`.
- **Tests that use it:** the waits-test and the stale-clear test wait on `turn.queued`. The ADD-dropped test waits on `queued` and then `abandoned`, which also removes the earlier vacuous-wait risk. All waits are bounded by `asyncio.wait_for(..., 5)`.
- **No private poke:** nothing reads `asyncio.Lock._waiters` any more.

Mutations ran only in `rsync` copies under `/private/tmp/claude-501/t6-scratch/`, and the worktree was never mutated.</result>

**Where the brief didn't fit as written:**

- **Item 3, eager start:** the shared read must start lazily. Started eagerly, a read that gets the turn at once stops being shareable before it was ever marked as shareable, and the next event joins a read that already has the turn.
- **Item 4:** I added a test that an ignored entry claiming a slot doesn't make it wanted or managed, because the "not ignored" half of the rule was otherwise untested. | # | SHA | What changed | Failed first in scratch |

| 1+2 | `53db7114` | One rule for a believed write that ends without the lock confirming it. | Two new ZHA end-to-end tests (details below). |

**Items 1+2: how the rule works.**
- Each read that finds the write not there yet now updates its last-seen value.
- Every path that ends a believed write puts the slot back to that value: given up, dropped as stale, or replaced by a clear. This is the new `_unbelieve`.
- A push now marks the data as the lock's word. Without that, a newer write the lock confirmed and pushed could have been undone when the older write was dropped. This has its own test.
- The breaker is still charged exactly once on give-up.

**Items 1+2: why I didn't take option (a).** Removing the slot's value from the data breaks re-sync. A managed slot missing from the data reads as unavailable, and the sync tick returns before it either charges the failed write or re-syncs. On a lock without polling, such as ZHA, nothing would read that slot again.

**Items 1+2: failing-first evidence.**
- Hole 1: the lock dropped the set, the first read-back found the slot empty, and later reads failed until the deadline. Giving up put back `known("1234")` instead of empty.
- Hole 2: a believed write of 4321 was dropped as stale when the PIN changed again. The data was left showing `known("4321")`.
- Mutation checks: each of the four mechanisms, when removed, fails its test.

**Item 3: details.** The test builds a quirk-style Door Lock cluster the way zha-quirks does (a `CustomCluster` subclass redefining `client_commands`). zigpy then compiles its own schema classes, distinct from the stock ones. Before the fix, that lock's set/clear success and failure replies were treated as unconfirmed, and its PIN read was unreadable: 5 test failures. The match also checks the command name, so a general command that happens to share an id is not taken as the answer. No test exercises that name check specifically.

**Item 4: no change.** `credentials` cannot be missing or None. HA's `lock_helpers._format_user_response` always builds a list (`for cred in (creds or [])`), and `LockUserData.credentials` is typed as a list. A Matter null reaches us as `[]`, so an empty list really means no credentials.

**Item 8: doesn't reproduce.** `async_confirm_pending_writes` already returns early on `if not self._pending`, with no await between that check and the read-back call. A scratch test settled the write by push before the look's first step and saw 0 probe calls and 0 reads. The probes run before the read-back waits for the lock's turn, so a write settled while it waits has already been probed. A coordinator re-check can't prevent that, and the existing in-turn empty check already handles it.</result>

**2+4 — retained replays (2c3afea3)**
- **What the provider relies on:** it can't drop every retained message. On a fresh subscription, the retained `userIdStatus` replays are what fill `_slot_enabled`, the gate that decides whether a code counts, and retained codes confirm slots.
- **The fix:** `message_received` passes `retained=msg.retain` down to `_process_user_code_value`. That function now ignores a retained value only when the slot has a write pending. Everything else is handled as before.
- **Tests:** the existing clear end-to-end test now fires its replay with `retain=True`, and I added a matching set test, `test_a_replayed_code_does_not_fail_a_set_that_landed`. A test helper, `fire_zui_node_value`, gained a `retain` keyword. The 1649267e test changes are reverted along with the code.
- **Failing first:** with the special case reverted and no provider fix, the clear test failed (2 clears charged instead of 1) and the set test failed (the pending write was settled as failed). Both pass with the fix.

**3 — a recorded failure survives a new write (ce805438)**
- **The fix:** `record_write`, `record_clear` and `drop_pending` no longer remove the slot from `_failed_writes`. A failure stays until the sync tick takes it.
- **No double charge:** `_failed_writes` holds at most one entry per slot and `take_failed_write` consumes it once, so a slot that failed twice is still charged once.
- **Tests:**
  - Coordinator: `test_record_write_keeps_an_earlier_failed_write_for_the_tick` takes the failure once, then gets False.
  - End-to-end: `TestAFailedClearsVerdict.test_a_clear_outside_the_tick_keeps_the_last_ones_verdict`.
  - Property test: `tests/properties/test_clear_charges.py` now models recorded-but-untaken failures separately from failures of clears still waiting. A waiting clear that gets superseded is dropped without a verdict.
- **Failing first:** the end-to-end test's `take_failed_write` returned False before the fix. Two encoding tests that expected the old discard were updated.

**1 — comment only (ae71d9fe)**
The comment now says what actually happens: where a code is expected, a stale Available status still lets a real or masked code be read. An empty or all-zeros code still resolves as empty there, and that half is left to the deferred #863 work.

**7 — status read once (ecdda1e4)**
- **The change:** in use is now `None if status is None else status == CodeSlotStatus.ENABLED`, computed from the status already read. `_uc_slot_in_use` is removed.
- **Why it's the same rule:** `get_usercode` (zwave_js_server `util/lock.py:103`) uses exactly that expression. Its only other path raises when the code value is missing. `Node.receive_event` creates the value before emitting, so the code value always exists by the time the handler runs.
- **Test:** `test_uc_shim_empty_code_follows_occupancy` now sends real `userIdStatus` events through the driver cache instead of patching the method, covering three cases: free, unknown and occupied.

**5 — Matter helper reuse (60da9d9f)**
- `_lcm_slot_from_raw_users_by_credential_index` finds the raw user that owns the PIN credential at that index and returns `_lcm_slot_of_raw_user(owner)`. I also spelled out the acronyms in its docstring.
- `_find_pin_credential_index_for_user` looks the user up with `_raw_user_by_user_index`, then returns the index of its first credential that passes `_is_pin_credential`.
- No behavior change: the full suite, including the Matter helper tests, is green with coverage still at 100%.

**Contradictions:** none of the findings failed to reproduce. One edge on item 5 you should know about:
- **The edge:** the old `_find_pin_credential_index_for_user` searched every raw user with a matching index, while the new one takes the first match. They would only differ if a lock listed the same user index twice and the first copy had no PIN.
- **Why it shouldn't happen:** HA's `get_lock_users` asks for strictly increasing indexes and copies back the `userIndex` from each response. A lock that follows the Matter spec returns the index it was asked for, so duplicates can't occur.
- **Even if it does:** the new behavior matches how the provider already looks up users in its two event handlers.</result>
