**3 — LockBusy clear is dropped (661e0db1)**
- **The fix:** `_dispatch_lock_user_change` catches `LockBusy` on its own. It logs at debug, pushes nothing and leaves the anchor. Transport and operation failures still fall back to the anchor, with the existing `anchored_at_event` check. The docstring and a comment say why.
- **New test:** `test_a_clear_whose_read_never_got_a_turn_is_dropped` covers the same-slot re-add. The anchor is `{42: 3}`, the clear's read queues behind a write that re-adds slot 3 (the lock hands back index 42), and the read gives up with LockBusy. It checks that nothing is pushed and the anchor is still `{42: 3}`.
- **Failing first:** before the fix it failed with `assert {} == {42: 3}`, because the fallback popped the anchor and pushed empty onto slot 3.

**4 — share the read until it asks the lock (a23ba1f9)**
- **The fix:** `pre_execute=_stop_sharing_this_read` is gone. The function run in the turn is now `_read_users_unshared`. Its first step lets go of the shared read, and that step runs after the rate-limit sleep. I checked that `_execute_locked` runs that function in the caller's own task, so the `current_task()` check still holds. The `_shared_user_read` docstring now says "has not yet asked the lock".
- **New test:** `test_an_event_during_the_reads_rate_limit_delay_shares_it` covers an event arriving while the read sits in the delay. It checks one `get_lock_users` call and that both slots are pushed.
- **Failing first:** before the fix it failed with "Awaited 2 times".
- **Round-4 guarantee kept:** the existing test for an event after the read has started still passes, renamed to `test_an_event_after_the_shared_read_has_started_reads_again`. All the other coalescing tests pass too.

**6 — AGENTS.md "Clears" (bab6243d)**
I read every provider's `async_delete_credential` and the base `async_internal_clear_usercode`. The bullet now says:
- **Push empty:** Matter, Zigbee Home Automation and zwave-js-ui push `SlotCredential.empty()` on a confirmed clear. Zigbee2MQTT does so once its publish succeeds.
- **Push nothing:** Z-Wave JS, because the driver clears its cached value and the push subscription reports it.
- **Just return True:** Virtual, Akuvox and Schlage.
- **When the refresh runs:** the base requests a refresh after any clear that changed something on a lock whose `supports_push` is False, whether or not the provider pushed. So a zwave-js-ui lock running api-only both pushes empty and is refreshed.

**Contradictions:**
- **An existing test asserted the old rule:** `test_a_clear_whose_read_never_got_a_turn_resolves_through_the_anchor` said a LockBusy clear should resolve through the anchor. Item 3 reverses that. I converted it to `test_a_clear_whose_read_fails_resolves_through_the_anchor`, which uses a transport error (`MatterClientException`). That keeps the remaining fallback covered and checks that the anchor is popped and empty is pushed.
- **The trade-off item 3 accepts:** a user deleted at the lock from outside Lock Code Manager, whose event's read waits out a long sync, is no longer pushed empty right away. It waits for the coordinator's next read to relearn the slot.
- **Failing-first method:** for items 3 and 4 I wrote each test before the fix and ran it in the worktree against the unchanged code. I didn't change any source to prove a failure.</result>

## Commits
- **`a06bb5d1` fix(zwave_js_ui): treat a Supervision Fail as a failed write.** A result with `status == Fail` (2) now raises `LockOperationFailed` on both set and clear, and nothing is pushed. Every other result behaves exactly as on main.
- **`97f6ec56` fix(zha): accept a get_pin_code reply only for the slot asked.** A reply counts only if it is a Get PIN Code Response naming the slot that was asked; anything else reads as `unreadable()`. The command is recognised by the id and name it was decoded as, not by its class, so a quirk's own classes still work. Plain lists are read by position only when they have at least 4 fields and name the asked slot, as on the reference branch. This commit also adds the ZHA e2e harness, `tests/providers/zha/helpers.py`, and updates `AGENTS.md`.
- **`1f400c01` fix(zha): take a write's status only from its own reply.** A status counts only from the write's own response, or from a Default Response whose `command_id` names the command just sent. A failure from those still raises `CodeRejectedError`. Everything else gets main's no-status behavior.
- **`de025dc2` fix(zwave_js): read an Available slot as empty (#819).** If the cached status is Available and no PIN is wanted on the slot, it reads empty. The status is checked before the "PIN wanted" predicate. That predicate is now one `@final` helper, `BaseLock._expects_pin`, used by the Z-Wave JS handlers and by the zwave-js-ui #863 check (no behavior change there). The status is now read once and `in_use` is derived from it, as reference `ecdda1e4` did.

## Failing first, on main's code
- **Fix 1:** both e2e tests failed because the refused write was pushed as the lock's state. The unit tests for Fail failed too. The parametrised non-Fail cases (Success, Working, NoSupport, no result, `{}`, boolean, string, list) pass on main, so they pin its behavior.
- **Fix 2:** both e2e tests failed with `KeyError: 1`: slot 1 was never programmed because another user's reply, or an operation event read by position, made it look in sync. The property test also fails against main's parser.
- **Fix 3:** three cases failed on main: a Default Response naming another command, a cluster command that shares the Default Response's id, and the clear-path equivalent. Real rejections pass on main and guard its behavior.
- **Fix 4:** the masked and leftover-code cases on an unwanted slot failed (read as `known("****")` and `known("5678")`). The wanted-slot, Enabled-with-code and occupied-empty cases pass on main.

Mutation checks ran in the scratch copy, restoring files in a `finally`. Every mutant was killed except one. That survivor is equivalent: the ZHA set and clear commands share their ids with their responses (5 and 7), so swapping `request.id` for `response.id` can't change anything. I kept `request.id` because a Default Response names the command that was sent.

## Main's no-answer behavior, kept
- **zwave-js-ui:** set pushes `known(pin)` and returns CONFIRMED; clear pushes `empty()` and returns True. Only an explicit Fail changes that.
- **ZHA:** main took any reply without a `.status` (a plain list, raw undecoded bytes, a reply with no status field) as success: set pushes `known(pin)` and returns CONFIRMED, clear pushes `empty()` and returns True. Every "not this write's answer" reply now gets exactly that, and there are no new OPTIMISTIC returns.

## Final results
- **Tests:** 2524 passed, 3 skipped, coverage 100% (7379 statements, 0 missed).
- **prek:** all hooks passed. The eslint hooks needed `yarn install` first.

## Where the brief didn't match the code
1. **Fix 1's comparison to Z-Wave JS only half holds.** There, a refused clear does become `LockOperationFailed`, but a refused set becomes `CodeRejectedError`, or OPTIMISTIC for `credential_rejected_unknown`. I followed the brief and used `LockOperationFailed` for both, and left the comparison out of the docstring.
2. **The boolean guard for fix 1 would never fire.** Fail is 2, and neither `True` (1) nor `False` (0) can equal it. I left the guard out, said why in the docstring, and kept the boolean test cases.
3. **ZHA write replies that are plain lists are never read as a status.** The brief mentions "a list that doesn't fit", but main never took a status from any list, and zigpy never returns plain lists for these replies. Reading a one-field list by position, as the reference did, would add a way to reject a code that main never had.
4. **The ZHA harness works at a different level from the reference.** Instead of mocking the cluster methods, the stand-in sits at `endpoint.request`. It decodes the frame zigpy built and sends back a frame decoded by the lock's own cluster, so tests get exactly what zigpy would return, quirk classes included. Old ZHA unit tests that hand-built reply objects without `user_id` now use zigpy's own reply classes.
5. **Not fixed, out of scope:** on Z-Wave JS, a masked code on a *Disabled* slot still reads as `known("****")`, the same mechanism as #819. Also, the "Config entry was never loaded" errors in ZHA test logs come from tearing down the ZHA entry itself; they predate this branch and only show when a test fails.

## Commits
- **`d3733008` (item 1):** a masked `userCode` now reads as `unreadable()` whatever the status. An Available slot with no code wanted still reads empty, because that rule runs first.

A Default Response is checked against zigpy's general Default Response class. This applies to the read path and both write paths. I added a quirk cluster that renames its responses.
- **`509ba4c3` (items 3+7):** Door Lock write statuses are mapped explicitly (table below). The commit message lists which statuses move from disabling the slot to the slot breaker.
- **`9d17a2d2` (item 8):** spelled out ZCL, LCM, JSON and PIN in comments and docstrings this branch added. A grep of every added line found no others.

## Failing-first evidence
- **Item 1:** a wanted slot with a stale Available plus `****` read `known('****')` before the fix.
- **Item 2:** with the renaming quirk, a lock already holding the configured codes got rewritten (its get replies read as unreadable), and a refusal in the renamed set response was confirmed as success.
- **Items 3+7:** five e2e cases failed on main because the slot was disabled: set status 1; Default Response naming the set with FAILURE, UNSUP_CLUSTER_COMMAND or NOT_AUTHORIZED; clear status 1, which also never charged the breaker. Memory-full and duplicate already disabled the slot on main, so those tests guard the existing behavior.
- **Items 5, 6, 8:** test-only, removal and doc changes, so no failing test applies.

Mutation checks ran in the scratch copy and every mutant was killed. That includes matching by name, matching by stock class, dropping the guard for a cluster that lacks the command, letting Default Responses take the Door Lock reasons, letting clear take the set's reasons, swapping duplicate for plain rejection, and treating success as a refusal.

## Status mapping versus main
On main, every non-zero status in a write's reply raised `CodeRejectedError`, which disables the slot.

| Reply | Main | Now |
|---|---|---|
| Set response, status 2 (memory full) | slot disabled | slot disabled, reason "the lock's memory is full" |
| Set response, status 3 (duplicate code) | slot disabled | slot disabled via `DuplicateCodeError` ("duplicate detected by lock firmware") |
| Set response, status 1 (general failure) | slot disabled | **slot breaker** (`LockOperationFailed`) |
| Set response, any status from 4 up | slot disabled | **slot breaker** |
| Clear response, any non-zero status | slot disabled | **slot breaker** |
| Default Response naming the set or clear, any non-success status | slot disabled | **slot breaker** |

Success, and any reply that isn't the write's own answer, are unchanged.

## Contradictions and judgment calls
1. **Clear statuses 2 and 3.** The brief maps 2 and 3 for "set/clear", but the Clear PIN Code Response only defines success and general failure. I send all non-zero clear statuses to the breaker. Covered by unit tests.
2. **Set statuses from 4 up** weren't in the brief. The cluster doesn't define them, so they go to the breaker.
3. **Default Responses carrying 2 or 3.** They hold zigpy's general status, not the Door Lock's, so they go to the breaker too. I added cases for these because no other test exercised that guard.
4. **Property-test oracle.** The property test used to judge replies by command name, which would just restate the new identification rule. It now judges each reply by how it was built and includes the renaming quirk.
5. **Item 6 checked before acting.** In the venv source, a pending ZHA request only ever receives a decoded command, raw bytes for an unknown command, or a `ParsingError`. The `list` in `Device.request`'s type is for Zigbee Device Object replies. No Door Lock quirk overrides `command`, `request` or `deserialize`, and quirks that make up replies return Default Responses. So nothing contradicted it and I didn't need to stop.
6. **One flaky run.** The first full run failed `tests/providers/test_base.py::test_rate_limiting_set_usercode`, a wall-clock check (`first_duration &lt; 0.2`) on the mock lock. It passed three times alone and in the next two full runs.

## What `is_slot_managed` does on main
It does not have the single-entry gap. It already calls `find_entry_for_lock_slot` across every entry, in any state, including disabled and ignored ones. Its only caller in `custom_components/` is `_check_duplicate_code` (for `conflicting_slot_managed`). Every-entry is exactly the meaning the brief wants for that flag.

Following the brief's literal instructions would mean switching `is_slot_managed` to `keeping_slots=True` and moving the duplicate check onto the default lookup. Production behaviour would be identical, and `is_slot_managed` would be left with no production callers. On the earlier branch it had a second caller, the `chargeable=` breaker code, which is out of scope here. So I left both untouched. Two consequences:
- **The "duplicate in a disabled entry's slot is reported as managed" test can't fail on main.** It already passes there, so I didn't add it.
- **The tests don't assert "not managed".** They check "wanted" only.

## The fix
- `domain/queries.py`: `find_entry_for_lock_slot` takes a keyword `keeping_slots: bool = False`. A new private `_keeps_its_slots(entry)` checks `disabled_by is None and source != SOURCE_IGNORE`. It ignores load state, and there is no fast path.
- `domain/coordinator.py`: `desired_credential` now uses `find_entry_for_lock_slot(..., keeping_slots=True)`. If no entry keeps the slot, it returns `SlotCredential.empty()`.
- The credential-used attribution caller keeps the every-entry default, so it behaves as before.
- There are three `desired_credential` callers. Besides `sensor.py` and `zwave_js_ui.py`, `providers/zwave_js.py:900` has the same #863 guard. All three are fixed by the one change.

## Tests, and how each failed on main

|---|---|

| The entry that created the lock is disabled while the second entry keeps the lock | Fails: slot 1 stays present instead of empty. |
| Entry not loaded yet: setting up, unloading for a reload, between unload and setup (3 cases) | All 3 fail: the slot goes empty instead of `known("5555")`. |
| Code sensor's unreadable fallback on the second entry's slot | Fails: shows `'unknown'` instead of `'2468'`. |

<result>I fixed it in one commit, `07f052d1`, on `fix/options-slot-overlap` (not pushed). The options flow now refuses a lock when another entry already manages some of this entry's slot numbers on it.

**Fix** (`/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/.worktrees/options-slot-overlap/custom_components/lock_code_manager/config_flow.py`, `async_step_init`): it runs `_check_common_slots(self.hass, user_input[CONF_LOCKS], config.slot_numbers, self.config_entry)` after the unclaimed-MQTT check and before the capacity check, the same way reauth does.

**Strings:** the options form's `error` section only had `unsupported_mqtt_lock`, so I added `slots_already_configured` with the setup flow's wording. That section was also missing `slot_out_of_range`, which the options flow's existing capacity check already returns, so users saw the bare key there. I added that too, as the same one-line gap on the same form. `strings.json` and `translations/en.json` are identical.

**Tests** (in `tests/test_config_flow.py`):
- **Overlap refused:** the other entry holds slots 1–3 on lock 2; this entry holds 1–2 on lock 1 and tries to add lock 2. The form shows `slots_already_configured` with `lock=lock.test_2`, `entry_title=other`, `common_slots="1, 2"`, and the entry's locks stay unchanged with no options saved.
- **Different numbers on a shared lock:** accepted.
- **Re-saving the entry's own locks:** accepted while the other entry holds the same numbers on a lock this one isn't adding. The entry doesn't conflict with itself.
- **String check:** a new helper asserts the options form defines each error key it returns and gets every placeholder the sentence names. I added it to the existing too-small-lock options test as well.

**Failing first:** before the fix, the overlap test failed because the flow returned `create_entry` instead of `form`. The too-small-lock test failed with `KeyError: 'slot_out_of_range'` in the options strings. The other two passed, as expected.

**Mutation checks** (in the scratch copy), all killed:
- With the check removed, the overlap test fails.
- With `None` passed instead of `self.config_entry`, 8 options tests fail, including the self-conflict test.
- With every slot on any shared lock treated as overlapping, the overlap and differing-numbers tests fail.

**Results:**
- Full suite: 2453 passed, 3 skipped, 100% coverage.
- `prek run --all-files` passes. The first run failed only because eslint wasn't installed; after `yarn install`, everything passed.

**User subentry and allocation paths:** a new user's slot number cannot collide with another entry's slot on a shared lock.
- Adding one user, adding several, and editing a user whose record has no number all go through `_allocate_for` → `async_allocate_for`. The first read always asks the lock about at least one number, and its result includes every number other entries manage on these locks (`claimed_by_other_entries`, from `get_managed_slots(..., excluding=config_entry)`). New users are numbered around those.
- Editing a user keeps their existing number.
- The one way a collision persists: tenure in `reconcile` keeps an existing number even if another entry also holds it, and its docstring says preventing that is the config flow's job. The options gap was the way such conflicts arose, so I left those paths unchanged.

**Where the brief didn't match the code:**
- The setup flow's user step doesn't call `_check_common_slots`; reauth is its only caller. Setup avoids other entries' numbers through allocation instead (see `test_numbers_another_entry_manages_are_stepped_over`). This didn't change the fix.
- Design choice for you: as with reauth, the check covers the whole submitted lock list, not just newly added locks. So an entry that already overlaps another on a lock, from before this fix, is refused on every lock edit until it drops that lock or the overlap is removed. The MQTT check waves through locks the entry already has. Here I chose to refuse, so the conflict surfaces. If you'd rather check only new locks, the change is filtering `user_input[CONF_LOCKS]` against `config.locks`.</result>

## Commits
- **`a03e7a1e`** `fix: an entry that failed keeps no slots on a shared lock` (items 2, 3 and 6)
- **`d42979f7`** `refactor: the code sensor asks its own entry for the desired code` (items 4 and 5)
- **`b85eea25`** `test(zwave_js_ui): a stale Available on a shared lock's second entry slot` (item 7)

## What changed
- **Item 2 (load states):** the venv's `ConfigEntryState` has exactly eight members, so there is nothing extra to place.
  - Keeping their slots: LOADED, SETUP_IN_PROGRESS, SETUP_RETRY, NOT_LOADED, UNLOAD_IN_PROGRESS. These are in a frozenset `_KEEPS_ITS_SLOTS_STATES` in `domain/queries.py`.
  - Not keeping them: SETUP_ERROR, MIGRATION_ERROR, FAILED_UNLOAD.
  - SETUP_RETRY now has its own reload-window case.
- **Item 3 (Home Assistant's filter):** `_keeps_its_slots` and the `SOURCE_IGNORE` import are gone. `find_entry_for_lock_slot` now calls `async_entries(DOMAIN, include_disabled=not keeping_slots, include_ignore=not keeping_slots)`, and our code only checks the state. I dropped the ignored-entry test.
- **Item 4 (dead attribute):** `self._config_entry` is removed from the coordinator. Nothing read it. The only `_config_entry` reader in the tests (`tests/test_sync.py:709`) is on the sync manager, not the coordinator.
- **Item 5 (sensor):** `desired_credential(address, *, owner=None)`. The sensor passes `owner=self.config_entry`; other callers still use the lookup. The enabled-and-PIN rule stays in one place. `ConfigEntry` has no `__bool__` or `__len__`, so `owner or …` is safe.
- **Item 6 (entry shape):** `_entry_on_slot_3` now builds entries as `data={CONF_LOCKS: [...]}` plus `subentries_data=user_subentries(...)`, the current shape.
- **Item 7 (zwave-js-ui end-to-end):** new class `TestSharedLock` in `tests/providers/zwave_js_ui/test_e2e.py`. The second entry gets slot 3 with PIN 2468 and syncs it to the table; then an MQTT `userIdStatus=AVAILABLE` arrives for slot 3. The test asserts the slot still holds the code and that no further write goes out over three sync ticks.

## Failing-first evidence (scratch copy, files restored in `finally`)
- **Excluded-state tests (setup_failed, migration_failed, unload_failed):** all 3 fail on `d3282b57`, the slot stays `known('5555')` instead of empty.
- **New SETUP_RETRY reload case:** fails on main (281745df).
- **zwave-js-ui test on main:** fails with the slot emptied (`present=False` vs `known('2468')`). With that assertion removed, it fails on the re-write too: `assert 2 == 1` writes.
- **Earlier tests, rebuilt in the current shape:** all still fail on main and pass now.

## Mutations
Caught:
- `include_disabled=True`: caught by the disabled-entry test.
- Dropping the state check: caught by all 3 excluded-state tests.
- Removing each kept state on its own: each caught by its own reload-window case.
- Making `from_entry`'s subentry branch drop slot numbers: caught by all four reload-window cases, both stale-Available end-to-end tests and the sensor test. So the reload-window cases do go through the subentry path.

Not caught, as expected:
- `include_ignore=True` survives because the ignored-entry test is gone. Without a discovery step an ignored entry can't exist, so this mutant changes nothing reachable.
- Dropping `owner=self.config_entry` in the sensor survives. Item 5 is a performance refactor: the lookup finds the same entry the sensor already holds. Only a test that inspects the call itself could tell them apart.

## Commits
- **`5d20d60c` (item 1):** a Default Response of `UNSUP_CLUSTER_COMMAND` or `UNSUP_MANUF_CLUSTER_COMMAND` naming the set or clear now raises `LockOperationUnsupported`. Every other failed Default Response is still `LockOperationFailed`.
  - Sync catches `LockOperationUnsupported` before `LockOperationFailed`. It suspends the slot on the first answer, with "cannot be synced because the lock will not accept it" followed by the error text. zwave_js raises it the same way, with a plain message (`_mapped_zwave_error`).
- **`434053b4` (item 2):** the write's own response is checked with `hasattr(result, "status")`, as main did. A response with no `status` field is no answer: the set is pushed and confirmed, and the clear is pushed empty.
- **`cd93c90c` (item 3):** a bytes code is decoded again with main's rule, `decode("utf-8", errors="ignore")`.
- **`994adb7e` (item 5):** each write is now one constant (`_SET_PIN_CODE`, `_CLEAR_PIN_CODE`) holding its command, its response, and a table from refusal status to the error it raises. Each call site is one line, and the `request == set_pin_code` comparison and the two status constants are gone. No test changed except the property test's call signature.
  - The `default_response` flag stays. A Default Response carries zigpy's general status codes, while the lock's own response carries Door Lock codes, so the same number means different things in each. I judged the change clearly simpler, so I made it.

## Failing-first evidence
- **Item 1:** the new e2e test (Default Response `UNSUP_CLUSTER_COMMAND` / `UNSUP_MANUF_CLUSTER_COMMAND` on a set) failed with no suspension issue: the refusal was retried as "refused without a reason about the code".
  - It now asserts the repair reason says "does not support set_pin_code" and names the status, the breaker count is 0, and the slot stays enabled.
  - The `unsupported_command` case moved out of the "left to the breaker" tests.
- **Item 2:** with a quirk-style cluster whose write responses name the field `result`, the unit test raised `AttributeError`. The e2e test hit sync's "Unexpected error ... Sync suspended" handler, and the enabled switch read `off` instead of `on`.
- **Item 3:** with a quirk-style cluster whose code field is `t.LVBytes`, the parse returned `"b'1234'"`, and the e2e lock already holding both configured codes had both slots rewritten.

Mutation checks for item 1 were all killed:
- The unsupported check also firing on the lock's own response: killed after I added an own-response case with value 0x81, which no test sent before.
- Never treating the command as unsupported: killed.
- Dropping `UNSUP_MANUF_CLUSTER_COMMAND`: killed.

Mutating items 2 and 3 would just restore the code their failing-first tests already failed on.

## Contradictions and notes
- Nothing in the brief contradicted the code.
- **Suite runtime:** the last full run took 55s against about 27s earlier on this branch. The machine's load average was about 340, and ZHA fixture setup took 2–4s per test instead of about 0.3s, so it's contention, not the tests.
  - The branch does about double the ZHA tests that set up a full lock: the four write tests run against three cluster types.
  - If you want the suite leaner, I can cut that parametrisation to the stock cluster and leave the quirk cases to the e2e tests.
- Declined #4 is not touched.

Commits added this round, after `9d17a2d2`: `5d20d60c`, `434053b4`, `cd93c90c`, `994adb7e`.</result>

<result>Round 3 is done in two commits. Items 1+6 and item 4 each got a fix with a test that failed first. Item 5 needed no change: every "PIN" the branch added is part of an official command name. The full suite passes in 26s (2567 passed, 3 skipped) at 100% coverage (7410 statements), and prek is clean. Nothing was pushed and `.entire/.gitignore` is still uncommitted. All work stayed in the `provider-responses` worktree even though the session's working directory moved to `shared-lock-entry`.

## Commits
- **`795ceb03` (items 1+6):** each write now reads its two kinds of answer through two separate tables. Its own response uses the Door Lock statuses, as before. A Default Response naming it uses zigpy's general statuses. Any status in neither table becomes `LockOperationFailed`, so nothing reaches a branch by fall-through.
  - The parsed answer carries the table that applies, which replaces the `default_response` flag. The raise is a single lookup with `LockOperationFailed` as the default.
  - The commit message has the before/after table against main, summarised below.
- **`7a31ccac` (item 4):** `user_id` and `code` are read the way main read them. A reply with no `user_id` isn't this slot's answer (unreadable), and one with no `code` holds no code (`getattr(result, "code", "")`), so an Available slot reads empty.

**Default Response naming the set, against main (main disabled the slot for every failure):**

| Status | Now |
|---|---|
| `DUPLICATE_EXISTS` | slot disabled (`DuplicateCodeError`) |
| `INSUFFICIENT_SPACE` | slot disabled ("the lock's memory is full") |
| `INVALID_VALUE`, `INVALID_FIELD` | slot disabled ("the lock reports the code invalid") |
| `UNSUP_CLUSTER_COMMAND`, `UNSUP_MANUF_CLUSTER_COMMAND` | slot suspended at once (`LockOperationUnsupported`) |
| any other failure | slot breaker (`LockOperationFailed`) |

**Default Response naming the clear:** only the two unsupported statuses suspend the slot; every other failure goes to the breaker. I found no reason to map the code-specific statuses for a clear: a clear carries no code, and the Clear PIN Code Response itself defines only success and general failure.

## Failing-first evidence
- **Items 1+6:** an e2e for each code-specific status (`DUPLICATE_EXISTS`, `INSUFFICIENT_SPACE`, `INVALID_VALUE`, `INVALID_FIELD`) failed. Each was retried as "refused without a reason about the code" instead of disabling the slot with the lock's reason.
  - The unsupported class and the "other" class (FAILURE, NOT_AUTHORIZED) already had e2e tests from earlier rounds.
  - A unit test pins the clear arm: those four statuses on a clear go to the breaker.
  - Mutation checks in scratch killed all seven mutants:
    - clear reading the set's Default Response table;
    - each kind of reply reading the other's table;
    - dropping `DUPLICATE_EXISTS` or `INVALID_FIELD` from the set's table;
    - swapping memory-full for invalid-code;
    - swapping duplicate for invalid-code.
- **Item 4:** with a quirk that names the code field `pin`, the e2e's Available slots read unreadable instead of empty. The unit tests for a missing `code` and a missing `user_id` raised `AttributeError`.

## Notes
- **`user_status` stays unguarded.** The brief didn't ask for it, and a reply missing it already ends as unreadable through the read loop's exception handler, which is the safe answer. Guarding it would add a branch with nothing to test.
- **Missing `user_id`:** at the provider level this was already unreadable before the fix, via the same exception handler. The behavior that actually changed is the missing `code` on an Available slot, and that is what the e2e test proves.
- No contradictions with the brief; the two declined items were left alone.</result>

| Slot-overlap check skipped | 4 |
